“At midnight she is at play,” said the favorite.
“I will put off my visit then till two in the morning,” replied Mangogul.

===Chinese===

同玛尼蒙班达搭伙的太太们，大半打牌打得火热；这不用曼戈古尔的精明也看得出来。赌这一种情欲，是最不懂得遮掩的。无论赢输，它都有剧烈的症候。“可这股疯狂打哪儿来？”他心里想。“太太们怎么受得了，整夜整夜围着法老牌桌，为等一张么点或一张七点发抖？这种癫狂伤她们的健康和容貌——倘若她们还有的话——更不消说它必定把她们拖进去的种种乱子了。”他凑到米尔佐扎耳边说：“我起了个念头，很想在这儿办一办。”

“你盘算的这好念头是什么？”宠妃说。

“是，”曼戈古尔答道，“把戒指转向这伙*赌婆*里最撒野的那个，审一审她的玩具，借那张嘴传一道好劝告，给天下那些软弱的丈夫——由着太太拿一家的名声和家当，去押一张牌、一颗骰子的丈夫。”

“这主意我太喜欢了，”米尔佐扎回道；“不过殿下可知道，玛尼蒙班达方才对着她的宝塔起了誓：她若再一次撞见腹语师的放肆，就再不开夜会了。”

“你说什么，我灵魂的欢喜？”苏丹插道。

“我用了，”宠妃答道，“端谨的玛尼蒙班达给所有玩具会说话的人起的名字。”

“那是她那蠢婆罗门造的词；他自诩懂希腊文，偏偏不㥯刚果话，”苏丹回道。“不过，只要玛尼蒙班达——还有她的司铎——肯赏脸，我倒想审一审玛尼拉（Manilla）的玩具；而且就在这里问才好，好叫左邻右舍都得点教化。”

“君主，”米尔佐扎说，“若依我，就省了玛尼蒙班达这一场不自在吧：这也误不了您的好奇，误不了我的。何不上玛尼拉家去？”

“你既这样说，我就去。”曼戈古尔说。

“可几点去？”苏丹娜说。

“半夜前后。”苏丹答。

“半夜她正在牌桌上，”宠妃说。

“那我把拜访推迟到凌晨两点。”曼戈古尔回道。

===Original===

“Prince, you don’t consider,” rejoined the favorite: “ ’tis the most pleasant hour of the four and twenty for female gamesters. If your highness will be led by me, you will take Manilla in her first sleep, between seven and eight.”
Mangogul followed Mirzoza’s advice, and visited Manilla about seven. Her women were going to put her to bed. He judged by the sadness predominant in her countenance, that she had play’d with bad luck. She walk’d to and fro, stopp’d, lifted her eyes to heaven, stamp’d with her foot, cover’d her eyes with her hands, and muttered somewhat, which the Sultan could not understand. Her women, who were undressing her, followed all these motions in panics; and if they at length compassed getting her into bed, it was not done without receiving harsh language and something worse. Now Manilla is in bed, having made no other night prayer, but some curses against a damned ace, which came seven times successively to her loss. Scarcely had she closed her eyes, when Mangogul levell’d his ring at her. Instantly her Toy exclaimed in a sorrowful tone: “Now I am repiqued and capotted.” The Sultan smiled to hear that everything about Manilla, even to her Toy, spoke gaming. “No,” continued the Toy, “I will never play against Abidul: he knows nothing but tricking. Speak to me no more of Dares: with him one runs the risk of some unlucky hits. Ismal is a pretty fair player, but everybody has him not that would. Mazulim was a treasure, before he fell into the hands of Crissa. I don’t know a more whimsical player than Zulmis. Rica is less so; but the poor lad is run dry. What can one do with Lazuli? The prettiest woman of Banza could not make him play high. What a piddling player Mollius is! In truth, desolation has spread among the gamesters: and e’er long we shall not know with whom to make a party.”
After this Jeremiad, the Toy fell to relating uncommon strokes, of which it had been a witness; and excessively extoll’d the constancy and resources of its mistress in bad luck.
“Were it not for me,” it said, “Manilla would have ruin’d herself twenty times over. All the Sultan’s treasures would not have discharged the debts that I have paid. In one match at brelan she lost to a farmer of the revenue and an abbé, above ten thousand ducats. She had nothing left but her jewels: but her husband had so lately redeem’d them, that she dared not to venture them.

===Chinese===

“君主，您没算计到，”宠妃又说：“那可是二十四小时里，女赌客最得意的时辰。殿下若肯听我的，就趁头一觉，七点八点之间去捉玛尼拉。”

曼戈古尔听了米尔佐扎的话，七点前后去访玛尼拉。她的女仆们正要服侍她上床。看她脸上压倒一切的愁容，苏丹断定她牌运不佳。她来回踱步，停下，眼望上天，跺脚，双手捂眼，嘴里叽咕着什么，苏丹听不懂。替她宽衣的女仆们，提心吊胆地跟着这一连串动作；她们好不容易把她弄上床，还挨了一顿恶话，外加更坏的什么。且说玛尼拉上了床，夜祷没做别的，只咒了一张该死的么点——它接连七回转到她手里叫她输。她刚阖眼，曼戈古尔把戒指对准了她。她的玩具登时哀声道：“这回我可又被满贯又被全收了。”听见玛尼拉浑身上下、连玩具都讲赌话，苏丹笑了。“不，”玩具接着说，“我再也不同阿比杜尔对局：他除了做手脚一无所长。达雷斯也再别向我提起：同他有一输就输到底的风险。伊斯马尔打得不坏，可意的人不都请得动他。马祖利姆本是个宝，直到落进克里萨手里。朱尔米斯的牌性，我认头一名的刁。里卡好些；可那可怜的家伙干了。拉祖利有什么办法？班扎顶漂亮的女人也激不起他的大牌。莫利乌斯那叫什么打法！说真的，赌客们一片荒凉：再过些日子，咱们连搭子的地方都寻不着了。”

发完这通哀歌，玩具又讲起它亲眼见过几桩不寻常的牌局，把他女主人背运里的镇定与手腕捧上了天。

“若不是有我，”它说，“玛尼拉早毁过二十回了。苏丹的全部宝藏，也抵不上我替她偿清的赌债。有一场布雷朗，她输给一个包税人和一位修道院长，一万多杜卡多。她只剩下珠宝；可她丈夫才赎回来不久，她不敢再拿去冒险。

===Original===

Nevertheless she took the cards, and had one of those seducing hands, which fortune sends, when she is on the point of cutting your throat. They urged her to speak. Manilla look’d at her cards, put her hand into her purse, whence she was sure to pull nothing, return’d to her cards, examined them again, but without determining.
“ ‘Does madam stand it at last?’ said the farmer.
“ ‘Yes; I stake,’ said she﻿—‘I stake﻿—I stake my Toy.’
“ ‘For what value?’ replied Turcares.
“ ‘For a hundred ducats,’ said Manilla. The abbé withdrew, esteeming the Toy too high rated. Turcares declared at it: Manilla lost and paid.
“The silly vanity of possessing a titled Toy got the better of Turcares. He offered to supply my mistress with play-money, on condition that I should be subservient to his pleasures. The bargain was struck in a moment. But as Manilla play’d high, and the farmer was not inexhaustible, we soon saw the bottom of his coffers.
“My mistress had appointed a most brilliant party at Pharaoh. All her acquaintance were invited. They were to punt with nothing under ducats. We depended on the purse of Turcares. But the morning of this great day, the rascal wrote us word that he had not a penny, and left us in the utmost consternation. However, we must extricate ourselves, and there was not a moment to be lost. We pitch’d on an old chief of the Bramins, to whom we sold at a dear rate some complaisances, which he had solicited a long time. This sitting cost him double the income of his ecclesiastical preferment.
“Turcares returned notwithstanding, in a few days. He said he was excessively concerned that madam had taken him at a nonplus: he still reckon’d on her goodness. ‘Indeed you reckon ill, my dear,’ replied Manilla: ‘I cannot with decency receive you more. When you were in a condition to lend, the world knew why I admitted you: but now that you are good for nothing, you would blast my honour.’
“Turcares was piqued at this discourse, and so was I: for he was perhaps the best lad in Banza. He waved his usual politeness, and gave Manilla to understand, that she cost him more than three opera girls, who would have amused him better, ‘Alas!’ cried he most mournfully, ‘why did I not stick to my little milliner? She loved me to folly. I made her so happy with a silk gown.’

===Chinese===

可她还是抓起了牌，偏偏抓到一手诱人的好牌——命运要割你喉咙之前，惯会送这种牌来。众人攛她开价。玛尼拉看看牌，手伸进钱袋——那里面她明知掏不出什么——又回到牌上，再看一遍，还是拿不定。

“‘夫人到底跟不跟？’包税人说。

“‘跟；我押，’她说——‘我押——我押我的玩具。’

“‘作价几何？’图尔卡雷斯（Turcares）回道。

“‘一百杜卡多。’玛尼拉说。修道院长退了，嫌玩具作价太高。图尔卡雷斯应了；玛尼拉输了，付了。

““占有一只领了封号的玩具”——这份虚荣压倒了图尔卡雷斯。他提出借给女主人赌本，条件是我得伺候他的快乐。买卖即刻成交。可玛尼拉押得大，包税人的钱袋也不是无底；不多时，我们便望见了他钱柜的底。

““女主人定了一场顶风光的法老局。相识的全请了。下注一杜卡多起步。全指望图尔卡雷斯的荷包。可大日子当天早晨，那无赖来信说一个子儿也没有了，把我们撂在极度的慌乱里。然而非脱身不可，一刻也耽误不得。我们挑中了一位年高的婆罗门首座，把几样他悬求已久的殷勤，卖了他一个天价。这一局，花掉他圣俸双倍的收入。

““过几天图尔卡雷斯还是回来了。他说，夫人叫他弄到绝地，他难过得要命；他仍旧指望她的好心。‘亲爱的，你指望错了，’玛尼拉回道：‘我再体体面面地接待不得你了。你借得起钱那阵，世人都知道我为什么让你进门：如今你一文不名，再进我的门就坏我的名声了。’

““图尔卡雷斯听了这话着恼，我也着恼：他兴许是班扎最好的小伙子。他把惯常的客气一撇，叫玛尼拉明白：她花掉他的，比三个歌剧歌女还多，歌女倒更会哄他。‘唉！’他惨声道，‘我怎么没守着我的小女帽商！她爱我爱得发疯。我送她一条绸裙，就哄得她那样幸福。’

===Original===

Manilla, who did not relish comparisons, interrupted him in a tone, enough to make one tremble, and bade him begone in an instant. Turcares knew her, and chose rather to return peaceably downstairs, than to leap through the window.
“After that, Manilla borrowed of another Bramin, who came, said she, to administer comfort in her afflictions. The holy man succeeded the farmer of the revenue, and we reimbursed him his comforts in the same coin. She lost me several times more, and ’tis well known that play-debts are the only ones that are paid among the Beau Monde.
“If Manilla happens to play with good luck, she is the most regular woman in Congo. Excepting her play, she takes surprising care of her conduct: she is never heard to swear an oath: she entertains well: she pays her mercer and other tradesmen, is liberal to her servants, redeems her knickknacks sometimes, and caresses her lap dog and her husband: but thirty times a month she risks these happy dispositions and her money on an ace of spades. Such is the life she leads, and will lead: and God knows how many times yet I shall be pawn’d.”
Here the Toy ceased, and Mangogul went to take repose. He was awaken’d at five in the afternoon, and went to the opera, according to a promise made to the favorite.

===Chinese===

玛尼拉最不吃比较这一套，抢断他的话，声调叫人发抖，命他即刻滚。图尔卡雷斯识得她，宁可安安稳稳从楼梯走下去，也不愿从窗户跳。

“此后玛尼拉又向另一个婆罗门借；那人是——她说——来在苦难中安慰她的。圣者接了包税人的班，我们也用同一枚硬币，偿清了他的安慰。她又把我输过好几回；而且谁都知道，赌债是上流社会里唯一必还的债。

““玛尼拉若赶上牌运好，便是刚果最规矩的女人。撇开赌不谈，她把操行照看得惊人地紧：从不听她起一个誓；待客殷勤；绸缎商和各铺子的账照付；下人赏得大方；小玩物有时也赎回来；还疼她的小哈巴狗和她的丈夫：可一月里三十回，她把这些好品性连同钱袋，一齐押在一张黑桃么上。这就是她过的日子，往后也还是这日子；天知道我还要被当掉多少回。”

玩具说到这里住了口，曼戈古尔去安歇。下午五点他被唤醒，依着对宠妃许下的诺言，看戏去了。

## XIII Of the Opera at Banza / 论班扎的歌剧
===Original===

Of all the public diversions of Banza, none supported itself but the opera. Utmiutsol and Utremifasolasiututut, two celebrated musicians, one of whom was growing old, and the other was but just new-fledged, alternately occupied the lyric scene. Each of these two original authors had his partisans. The ignorant and the grey-bearded dotards stood up for Utmiutsol; the smart young fellows and the Virtuosi were for Utremifasolasiututut: and the people of taste, as well young as old, held them both in high esteem. Utremifasolasiututut, said the latter, is excellent when he is good, but he sleeps at times; and, pray, to whom does not that happen? Utmiutsol holds up better, and is more uniform. He is full of beauties; yet he has not one, of which there are not examples to be found, and even more striking, in his rival; in whom there are strokes to be observed, which are entirely his own, and are not to be met with anywhere but in his works. Old Utmiutsol is simple, natural, smooth, sometimes too smooth, and that is his fault. Young Utremifasolasiututut is singular, brilliant, composed, learned, sometimes too learned: but perhaps that is his hearer’s fault. The one has but one opening, beautiful indeed, but repeated at the head of all his pieces. The other has made as many openings as pieces, and they all pass for masterpieces. Nature guided Utmiutsol in the ways of melody; study and experience discovered the sources of harmony to Utremifasolasiututut. Who ever knew how to declaim, and who will ever speak a part like the old man? Who will compose light catches, voluptuous airs, and symphonies in character like the young one? Utmiutsol is the only person who understood dialogue. Before Utremifasolasiututut nobody distinguished the delicate touches, which separate the tender from the voluptuous, the voluptuous from the passionate, the passionate from the lascivious. Nay some partisans of the latter pretend, that if Utmiutsol’s dialogue is superior to his, this is not so much owing to the inequality of their talents, as to the difference of the poets, whom they made use of. “Read, read,” cried they, “the scene of Dardanus, and you will be convinced, that if we give good words to Utremifasolasiututut, Utmiutsol’s charming scenes will be revived.”

===Chinese===

班扎的一切公众娱乐里，只有歌剧自立得住。乌特米乌特索尔同乌特雷米法索拉西乌图图特（Utremifasolasiututut），两位大名鼎鼎的音乐家——一个渐渐老了，一个羽翼方新——轮流占着抒情舞台。这两位独创的作家各有拥趸。无知之辈和白胡子老糊涂捧乌特米乌特索尔；伶俐的小伙子和鉴赏行家拥乌特雷米法索拉西乌图图特：而有品味的人，不论老少，对两位一样推重。后一派说：乌特雷米法索拉西乌图图特好起来精彩绝伦，只是时有昏睡；可谁保得住不犯这个呢？乌特米乌特索尔更稳，更匀。他满纸妙处；然而没有一处，在他的对手那里找不到先例，甚至更惊人；而对手笔下有几笔，纯是他自家的，除他的作品外别处遇不着。老乌特米乌特索尔简洁、自然、流畅，有时太流畅了，这是他的毛病。年轻的乌特雷米法索拉西乌图图特奇特、辉煌、沉稳、有学问，有时太有学问：不过那兴许是听客的毛病。一位只有一个序曲，固然美，却安在他每一部作品的头上；另一位写了多少部就有多少个序曲，个个被誉为杰作。自然领着乌特米乌特索尔走旋律的路；钻研与阅历为乌特雷米法索拉西乌图图特揭示了和声的源头。谁曾经会朗诵、往后谁还能像老头子那样念台词？谁又能像年轻的这位，谱轻快的叠歌、艳丽的咏叹、合体的配乐？乌特米乌特索尔是唯一懂得对话的人。乌特雷米法索拉西乌图图特之前，没有人分辨得出那些细腻的笔触：温柔与艳冶之间、艳冶与热烈之间、热烈与淫荡之间的分界。不但如此，后者的拥趸里还有人说：乌特米乌特索尔的对话若胜过他，未必因为两人才分悬殊，多半因为他们用的诗人不同。“读一读，读一读《达达努斯》那场戏，”他们喊，“你们就会信：只要给乌特雷米法索拉西乌图图特好词儿，乌特米乌特索尔那些迷人的场面就能复活。”

===Original===

However that be, in my time, the whole town flock’d to the tragedies of the latter, and people stifled one another at the interludes of the former.
They were just then exhibiting in Banza an excellent piece of Utremifasolasiututut, which would never have been represented but in nightcaps, had not the favorite Sultana had the curiosity to see it. And besides, the periodical indisposition of Toys favored the jealousy of the fiddles, and made the principal actress flinch. She, who supplied her place, had not so good a voice, but as she made amends by her manner of acting, nothing hindered the Sultan and the favorite from honouring the piece with their presence.
Mirzoza was already come, Mangogul comes, the curtain is raised, they begin. Everything went on marvellously well: Miss Chevalier had effaced the memory of Miss le Maure, and they were at the fourth act, when the Sultan bethought himself, in the middle of a chorus, which he thought lasted too long, and had already cost the favorite two yawns, to point his ring on all the singers. Never was there seen on the stage so odd and comical a sight. Thirty women were struck dumb on a sudden. Their mouths were wide open, and they kept the same theatrical attitudes they held before. And at the same time their Toys made their throats sore with the violence of singing, this a “Pont-neuf,” that a “Vaudeville polisson,” another a very indecent parody, and all of them extravagances relative to their characters. On one hand was heard, *oh vraiment ma comere, oui*; on the other, *quoi douze fois?* Here, *qui me baise, est-ce Blaise?* There, *rien, Père Cyprien, ne vous retient*. *Infine*, they all wound themselves up into a strain, so high, so rude, and so mad, that they formed the most extravagant brawling ridiculous choir, that had been heard before and since that of —﻿—﻿no﻿—﻿—﻿d﻿—﻿—﻿on﻿—﻿—﻿
---
Here the manuscript was worm-eaten.
---
All this while the Orchestra went on, and the loud laughs of the pit and boxes joined to the sound of the instruments, and the singing of the Toys, completed the cacophony.
Some of the actresses, fearing lest their Toys, tired with quavering impertinences, might resolve to speak them out, ran behind the scenes: but they escaped with the fright only. Mangogul being persuaded that the public would learn nothing new, turn’d off his ring.

===Chinese===

无论怎样，在我那个时候，全城人挤破头去看后者的悲剧，看前者的幕间歌舞时，人挤得喘不上气。

其时班扎正上演乌特雷米法索拉西乌图图特的一部杰作；若不是宠妃苏丹娜好奇要看，这部戏本会同睡帽一起埋没了。而且，多亏玩具们周期性的喑哑，也助长了提琴师们的醋意，叫女主角怯了场。顶替她的那位嗓子差些，好在拿做派补了；苏丹同宠妃赏光驾临，也就没有什么拦着的了。

米尔佐扎先到，曼戈古尔随后，幕启，开演。一切进行得妙极：谢瓦利耶小姐抹掉了勒莫尔小姐的记忆，戏已到第四幕，苏丹在一支合唱里忽然起了念头——那合唱他嫌太长，已经劳宠妃打了两个呵欠——把戒指对准了全体歌者。舞台上从未见过这样古怪滑稽的场面。三十个女人霎时齐哑。嘴大张着，先前的舞台姿势一一保持不动。与此同时，她们的玩具唱得嗓子都哑了：这只来一支《新桥小调》，那支来一支“浪荡沃德维尔”，另一支唱不堪入耳的仿作，唱的全是同各自角色相称的荒唐货。这边一句*“哦真的吗，我的老姐妹，是吗”*；那边一句*“什么，十二回？”*；这里*“亲我的是谁，可是布莱兹？”*；那里*“什么也拦不住你，西普里安神父”*。*末了*，个个拔到一个又高、又野、又疯的调门上，汇成了有史以来最荒唐、最聒耳、最好笑的合唱队——比——不——的——那支——

---

写本在此被虫蛀了。

---

这半天，乐队照奏不误；正厅和包厢的哄笑，和着乐器的声响与玩具们的歌唱，凑足了这场不谐和的大杂烩。

有几个女演员生怕自己的玩具颤音唱累了无礼话，索性开口明说，逃到了后台：不过她们只受了场虚惊。曼戈古尔料定公众听不出什么新东西，把戒指转了过去。

===Original===

In an instant all the Toys were silent, the bursts of laughter ceased, the audience grew calm, the play was resumed and finished peaceably. The curtain drop’d, the Sultana and Sultan went off, and the Toys of our actresses went to their several appointments, where they were to have a different employment from singing.
This affair made a great noise. The men laugh’d, the women were alarmed, the Bonzas were scandalized, the academicians rack’d their brains about it. But what did Orcotomus say? Orcotomus triumph’d. He had hinted in one of his memoirs, that the Toys would infallibly sing: they had sung accordingly, and this phenomenon, which disconcerted his brethren, was to him a new ray of light, and completed the confirmation of his system.

===Chinese===

霎时间众玩具齐哑，哄笑停了，观众安静下来，戏接着演，平平安安演到完。幕落，苏丹娜同苏丹离场，咱们女演员们的玩具各自赴约去了——在那里，它们另有差事，不比唱歌。

## XIV Orcotomus’s Experiments / 奥尔科托穆斯的实验
===Original===

It was on the fifteenth of the moon of —﻿—﻿—, that Orcotomus had read his memoir to the academy, and communicated his thoughts on the tattle of the Toys. As he then did with the greatest assurance promise to produce infallible experiments, which he had repeated several times, and always with success; the majority of people was struck with his doctrine. For some time the public preserved the favorable impressions they had received: and for full six weeks Orcotomus pass’d for having made considerable discoveries.
Nothing was wanting to complete his triumph, but performing before the academy the famous experiments, which he had preached so loudly. The assembly that met on this occasion was extremely brilliant. The ministers of state graced the meeting: the Sultan himself did not disdain to be there, but invisible.
As Mangogul was a great Monologue maker, and the futility of the conversations of his time had tainted him with the habitude of soliloquy: “Either Orcotomus,” said he, “must be an arrant quack, or the Genius my protector, a great fool. If the academician, who certainly is not a conjurer, can restore speech to dead Toys; the Genius who protects me was in the wrong to make a compact, and sell his soul to the devil, for the sake of communicating it to Toys full of life.”
Mangogul was puzzling himself with such reflections, when he found himself in the middle of his academy. Orcotomus had for spectators, as appears, all those in Banza, who were knowing in the subject of Toys. In order to be satisfied with his audience, all he wanted was to give them satisfaction: but the issue of his experiments proved very unhappy. Orcotomus took up a Toy, put his mouth to it, blew into it till he was out of breath, quieted it, returned, tried another: for he had brought a variety of them with him, of all ages, sizes, conditions and colours: but in vain did he blow; nothing was heard but inarticulate sounds, vastly different from what he had promised.
Then ran a buzzing murmur through the company, which disconcerted him for a moment: but he recovered himself, and alleged that such experiments were not easy to be made before so great a number of people: and he was right.
Mangogul got up in wrath, went away, and in the twinkling of an eye was in the favorite Sultana’s apartment. “Well, prince,” said she upon seeing him, “who has won the day, you or Orcotomus?

===Chinese===

在——月的月圆后第十五日，奥尔科托穆斯向学院宣读了他的论文，陈述了他对玩具闲话的见解。他当日拍着胸脯担保，说他掌握必成的实验，做过好几回，回回灵验；大多数人便被他的学说慑住了。有好一阵，公众保留着好印象：整整六个星期，奥尔科托穆斯被当作是有了重大发现的人物。

要成全他的凯旋，只差当着学院把那些他吹得震天响的著名实验做出来。为此召集的盛会极一时之盛。国务大臣们到场生色：苏丹本人也不屑于屈尊到场——只是隐着身。

曼戈古尔惯做独白，他那时代谈话的空疏又染了他一身自言自语的习惯。“奥尔科托穆斯，”他说，“若不是个十足的国医，便是护我的精灵是个大傻瓜。这位院士——他分明不是术士——尚能叫死玩具开口；那护我的精灵，为了把这门本事传给活玩具，就实在不该立约，把灵魂典给魔鬼。”

曼戈古尔正想着这些，人已到了学院中央。看客里，显然有班扎全城懂玩具这一门的行家。他要观众满意，只消把观众满意拿出来；可他的实验，下场极惨。奥尔科托穆斯抓起一只玩具，嘴对上去，吹得气都接不上，把它安抚下来，回头再换一只试：他带来的样品很齐全，各年龄、各尺寸、各身分、各色相都有：可吹也是白吹；只听见一些不成音节的响动，同他许下的大话相去天壤。

会众里响起一片嗡嗡，一时把他搅慌了：可他随即定住，申辩说这类实验，在这么多观众面前不好做：这话倒也在理。

曼戈古尔怒而起身，走了；一眨眼已到宠妃苏丹娜的房里。“嗯，君主，”她一见他便说，“谁赢了，您还是奥尔科托穆斯？

===Original===

For his Toys have performed wonders to be sure.” The Sultan took some turns about the room without a word of answer. “But,” resumed the favorite, “your highness seems dissatisfied.”
“Oh! madam, the impudence of that Orcotomus is not to be match’d. Pray, do not so much as mention him from this moment﻿—What will you say, O future generations, when you shall be informed, that the great Mangogul allowed a hundred thousand crowns in yearly pensions to such fellows; while gallant officers, who with their blood had watered the laurels that surround his brow, were reduced to twenty pound a year?﻿—By Jove, the thought distracts me: I shall be out of humor this month to come.”
Here Mangogul broke off, and continued to walk about the room. He hung down his head, marched to and fro, stop’d, and now and then stamp’d the floor with his foot. He sat down a moment, rose up in a hurry, took his leave of Mirzoza, forgot to kiss her, and retir’d to his own apartment.
The African author, who has immortalized himself by the history of the high and marvellous exploits of Erguebzed and Mangogul, continues in these words:
By Mangogul’s ill humor it was thought, that he was on the point of banishing all the learned out of his dominions. Far from it, the next day he arose in a gay mood, performed his exercises at the riding-house in the morning, in the evening supp’d with Mirzoza and some favorites, under a magnificent tent in the gardens of the Seraglio, and never appeared more disengaged from affairs of state.
The dissatisfied and the disaffected of Congo, and the newsmongers of Banza, did not fail to spread their reports of this conduct. For what do not people of this stamp find fault with? “Is this,” said they in the public walks and coffeehouses, “is this governing a state? To spend the day in tilting, and the night at table.”
“Well, if I was Sultan,” cried a little Senator ruined by gaming, parted from his wife, and whose children had the worst of education, “if I was Sultan, I would make Congo a flourishing empire. I would be the terror of my enemies, and the darling of my subjects. Within six months I would reestablish the Police, the laws, the army and the navy in their full vigour. I would have a hundred ships of the line. Our heaths should soon be grub’d up, and our highways repair’d. I would abolish the taxes, or at least reduce them to one half.

===Chinese===

他的玩具们想必是显了圣了。”苏丹在屋里绕了几圈，一字不答。“可是，”宠妃又说，“殿下像是不痛快。”

“哦！夫人，这奥尔科托穆斯的脸皮，天下无双。从此刻起，请再不要对我提他——啊，后世的人们，待你们听说，伟大的曼戈古尔年年给这号人物发十万克朗的俸金，而拿热血浇灌他冠上桂冠的骁勇军官们，一年却只落得二十镑，你们会说什么？——朱庇特在上，这念头搅得我发昏：这一个月里，我的脸色都好不了。”

说到这里，曼戈古尔打住，接着在屋里踱。他垂着头，走来走去，停下，不时跺一跺地板。坐了一霎，又忽地起身，向米尔佐扎告辞，忘了吻她，回自己房里去了。

这位因埃尔格贝泽德与曼戈古尔的崇高奇迹史而不朽的非洲作者，接下去这样写道：

看曼戈古尔这副坏脾气，人都以为他就要把学者统统驱逐出境了。差得远呢；次日他兴冲冲起了床，早晨在骑术馆练了功课，晚上同米尔佐扎和几位宠臣在御花园一顶华丽的帐幄下用饭，从没有显得这样丢得开国务。

刚果的不平之徒、不满之徒，同班扎的包打听们，少不了拿这一番行止去铺排他们的传闻。这一路人还有什么挑剔不出的？“这算是，”他们在公共散步道和咖啡馆里说，“这算是治国吗？白天耍枪，夜里赴席。”

“喏，我要是苏丹，”一个叫赌钱败光了、被老婆分了居、孩子也没教管好的小元老喊道，“我要是苏丹，我叫刚果变个繁盛的帝国。我叫敌人怕我，臣民爱我。不出半年，我把巡警、法度、陆军、海军一律恢复旧观。我要一百艘战列舰。咱们的荒地不日开垦，官道不日修齐。我把捐税废了，至少砍去一半。

===Original===

As for pensions, gentlemen of sublime wit, by my faith, ye should but just taste them with the tip of your tongues. Good officers, Pongo Sabiam, good officers, old soldiers, magistrates like us, who devote our labours and night studies to dealing out justice to the people; these are the men on whom I would shed my bounty.”
“Gentlemen,” said an old toothless politician, with greasy flat hair, a coat worn out at elbows, and ragged ruffles, “have ye quite forgot our great emperor Abdelmalek, of the Dynasty of the Abyssinians, who reigned two thousand three hundred and eighty five years ago? Have ye forgot how he caused two astronomers to be impaled, for an error of three minutes in their prediction of an eclipse; and his first physician and surgeon to be dissected alive, for having ordered him a dose of manna at an improper time?”
“Moreover I ask you,” continued another, “what are those idle Bramins good for, that vermin who suck our blood, and grow fat on it. Would not their immense overflowing riches better become honest folks like us.”
From another quarter was heard: “Forty years ago, were the new cookery and the liquors of Lorraine so much as known? Our rulers are plung’d deep into luxury, which threatens approaching destruction to the empire, a necessary consequence of the contempt of the Pagodas and dissolution of morals. At the time when people eat but coarse meats, and drank but sherbet at Kanaglou’s table; what regard would be paid to the cut-paper ornaments, to Martin’s varnishes, and to Rameau’s music? The opera girls were not more cruel then than at present, and were to be had much cheaper. The prince, you see, spoils many good things. But if I was Sultan﻿—”
“If thou wert Sultan,” answered in wrath an old officer, who had escaped the dangers of the battle of Fontenoy, and had lost an arm close to his prince in the fight of Laufelt; “thou wouldst commit greater impertinences than thou now dealst out. Eh, friend, thou can’st not govern thy tongue, and thou wouldst willingly rule an empire: thou hast not sense to manage thy family, and thou pretendst to guide a state.

===Chinese===

至于年俸嘛，高才的诸位先生，凭良心说，你们也只配拿舌尖舔一舔。好军官，蓬戈·萨比阿姆在上，好军官、老丘八、还有像咱们这样的法官——把辛劳和夜课全搭进去，给百姓断案——才是我施恩的对象。”

“诸位先生，”一个没了牙的老政客说，头发平塌油腻，外套肘部磨穿，花边褴褛，“你们莫非忘了咱们的伟大皇帝阿卜杜勒马利克，阿比西尼亚王朝的那位，两千三百八十五年前御极的？忘了他怎样因为两位天文学家推算日食错了三分钟，便把他们钉了尖桩；又因为他的御医御外科在不当的时候给他开了一剂甘露，便叫人活体解剖了他们？”

“再者我问你们，”另一个接若说，“那帮吃闲饭的婆罗门有什么用？那群吸血、吸肥了自我的害虫。他们那漫出来的偌大家私，难道不更配咱们这样的正派人么。”

另一头有人喊：“四十年前，新式烹饪和洛林的酒可有人识得？咱们的执政者一头扎进奢侈里，眼看要断送帝国——那是不敬宝塔、风俗败坏的必然下场。想当年人们只吃粗肉、在卡纳格卢席上只喝果子露的时节，剪纸的装点、马丁的漆、拉莫的音乐，谁稀罕？那时候歌剧歌女不比如今更薄情，价钱还便宜得多。君主，您瞧，糟蹋了多少好东西。我要是苏丹——”

“你要是苏丹，”一位老军官怒声接道——他逃过丰特努瓦一役的刀山，又在劳费尔德一战里紧挨着君王丢了一条胳膊——“你放的无礼还要比现在多。嗳，朋友，你连自己的舌头都管不住，倒想管一个帝国：你连家都当不好，倒要来领一个国。

===Original===

Respect the powers of the earth, and thank the Gods for having given thee birth in the empire, and in the reign of a prince, whose prudence instructs his ministers, and whose soldiery admire his valor; who has made himself dreaded by his enemies, and beloved by his people; and whose only fault is the lenity with which persons of thy stamp are treated under his government.”

===Chinese===

敬畏地上的权柄吧，感谢诸神，生你在这帝国、在这位君主的治下：他的审慎教诲着他的大臣，他的勇武为他麾下的士卒所钦仰；他叫敌人畏惧，叫人民爱戴；他唯一的过失，就是把治下你这号人物，管束得太宽了。”

## XV The Bramins / 婆罗门们
===Original===

After the learned had spent themselves on the Toys, the Bramins took possession of them. Religion laid claim to their chitchat, as a subject that came within its jurisdiction, and its ministers pretended that the hand of Brama manifested itself in that work.
There was held a general assembly of Pontiffs, and a resolution was taken to employ the best pens to prove in form, that the thing was supernatural, and that, till their works could be published, it should be defended by way of theses in the schools, in private conversations, in the direction of consciences, and in public harangues.
Now, although they unanimously agreed that the thing was supernatural; yet as the people of Congo admitted two principles, and professed a sort of Manicheism, they were divided in opinion, to which of these two principles the chitchat of the Toys ought to be attributed.
Those who had seldom or never been out of their cells, and had turned over nothing but their books, ascribed the prodigy to Brama. “There is none but he,” said they, “that can interrupt the course of nature; and time will show, that in all this he has most profound views.”
Those on the contrary, who frequented the toilets, and were oftener surprised at bedsides than in their closets, fearing lest some indiscreet Toy or other might unveil their hypocrisy, laid their prattle to the charge of Cadabra, a mischief-making deity, and a sworn enemy to Brama and his servants. This latter system was exposed to terrible objections, and had not so direct a tendency towards the reformation of morals. Nay, its defenders did not impose on themselves about it. But the business was, to screen themselves: and in order to compass that, religion had not a single minister, who would not have sacrificed the Pagodas and their Altars a hundred times over.
Mangogul and Mirzoza assisted regularly at the religious service of Brama, and the whole empire was informed thereof by the newspapers. On a certain day appointed for celebrating one of the principal solemnities in the great Mosque, they were present. The Bramin, whose turn it was to explain the law, mounted the rostrum, pour’d forth a volley of affected phrases and fulsome compliments on the Sultan and his favorite; and made an eloquent peroration on the manner of sitting orthodoxly in company.

===Chinese===

学者们在玩具身上把力气使尽之后，婆罗门们把玩具接了过去。宗教声称，玩具的闲话归它管辖；它的教士们断言，这件事里显的是婆罗摩亲手。

于是召集了教长全体大会，议决动用最好的笔杆，正式证明此事系超自然；并决定在他们的论著刊行之前，先以学院论题、私下谈话、良心指导、公开讲道诸形式加以捍卫。

如今，他们虽一致认这事系超自然，但因刚果人承认两个本原，信一种摩尼教，他们就分了意见：玩具的闲话，该归在这两个本原哪一 个名下。

那些难得或从不迈出斗室、翻来翻去只有书本的，把这奇迹归给婆罗摩。“只有他，”他们说，“能打断自然的行程；日子会显出，这一切里面藏着他的至深用意。”

相反，那些常在梳妆台边走动、被人从床头抓住比从书房抓住的次数更多的，生怕哪只不客气的玩具揭他们的假，便把这套聒噪记在卡达布拉名下——那是专会作恶的神，婆罗摩及其仆人的死敌。后一学说招来可怕的驳难，也较少直接引导风俗向善。说真的，它的护法们自己也心里有数。可是要紧的是护住自身：为达到这一层，宗教的教士里，没有一个不肯把宝塔和祭坛牺牲上一百回的。

曼戈古尔和米尔佐扎按常去婆罗摩的礼拜，这件事由报纸传遍了帝国。到了在大清真寺庆祝主要节庆之一的指定日子，两人都在座。轮到讲律法的那位婆罗门登上了讲坛，朝苏丹和宠妃倾泻了一通矫饰的词藻和肥腻的恭维；随后就“在社交场合如何正统地坐”发表了一篇雄辩的结语。

===Original===

He had already demonstrated the necessity thereof by numberless authorities, when being instantaneously seized with a fit of sacred enthusiasm, he spoke this declamation, which had the greater effect, as it came in quite unexpected.
“What do I hear in all assemblies? a confused murmur, an unheard noise strikes my ears. All things are turn’d topsy-turvy, and the faculty of speech, which the goodness of Brama had hitherto appropriated to the tongue, is now by an effect of his vengeance transported to other organs. And what organs? You know, gentlemen. O ungrateful people, was there a necessity for a new prodigy, to rouse ye from your lethargy; and had not your crimes witnesses enough, but their principal instruments must raise their voice? Doubtless their measure is filled up; since the wrath of heaven has sought new chastisements. In vain did you envelop yourself in darkness, in vain did you choose mute accomplices: do you not hear them at present? They have given depositions against ye from every quarter, and revealed your baseness to the universe. O thou who governest by thy wisdom! O Brama! just are thy judgments. Thy law condemns theft, perjury, lying and adultery: it proscribes the blackness of calumny, the intrigues of ambition, the fury of hatred, and the artifices of insincerity. Thy faithful ministers have not ceased declaring these truths to thy children, and threatening them with the chastisements, which thou in thy just anger reservedst for prevaricators: but in vain; the fools have delivered themselves up to the tide of their passions, they followed the torrent, they have despised our admonitions, they have laughed at our threats, they have regarded our anathemas as empty noise; their vices are accumulated, strengthen’d, multiplied; the voice of their impiety has ascended even to thy throne, and we have not been able to prevent the tremendous scourge, with which thou hast smote them. After having long implored thy mercy, let us now extoll thy justice. Overwhelmed by thy strokes, doubtless they will return to thee, and acknowledge the hand which is laid heavy on them. But O prodigy of hardheartedness! O excess of blindness! They have imputed the effect of thy power to the blind mechanism of nature. They have said in their hearts, Brama is not.

===Chinese===

他已引经据典把这样坐的必要性论证了无数条，忽然一阵神圣的热情攫住了他，他诵出下面这篇檄文；因为来得全无预兆，效果反倒加倍。

“我在一切集会里听见什么？一片嘈杂的嗡嗡，一种未之前闻的声浪震我耳鼓。万事都翻了了个儿；言语之能，婆罗摩的恩慈原只授给舌头，如今凭他的震怒，挪到了别的器官上。什么器官，诸位自知道。哦，负恩的百姓啊！难道非得再来一件奇迹，才唤得醒你们的迷睡？难道你们的罪孽，证人还不够，连罪的主要工具也要开口？无疑，他们的恶贯已盈；上天的怒火这才另寻新的惩罚。你们藏进黑暗，白搭；你们挑哑巴同谋，白搭：如今你们听不见它们么？它们已从四面八方出首告发，把你们的卑劣揭给了宇宙。哦，以智慧御世的你！哦，婆罗摩！你的审判是公义的。你的律法谴盗窃、谴伪誓、谴谎言、谴奸淫：它放逐诬蔑的黑、野心的诡计、仇恨的狂、虚伪的诈。你忠实的仆人，从未停止把这些真理宣示给你的儿女，以你公义的愤怒为渎职者备下的刑罚警告他们：可是白搭；蠢人们把自己交付给情欲的潮水，随流而下，轻看我们的训诫，哂笑我们的恫吓，把我们的诅咒当空响；他们的罪恶堆积起来，加固了，繁衍了；他们不敬的声音已升到你的宝座前，我们终究没能拦下你加于他们的可怕的鞭子。长久哀求你的慈患之后，如今让我们称颂你的公义。被你的打击压倒了，他们必归向你，必认出那重重按在他们身上的手。可是，哦，心硬的奇迹！哦，瞎眼的极点！他们竟把你权能的效验，归给自然的盲动。他们心里说：婆罗摩不存在。

===Original===

All the properties of matter are not known to us, and the new proof of its existence is but a proof of the ignorance and credulity of those, who object it to us. On this foundation they have raised systems, invented hypotheses, tried experiments: but from the height of his eternal habitation, Brama has laugh’d at their vain projects. He has confounded audacious knowledge, and the Toys have broke, as glass, the impotent obstacles opposed to their loquacity. Wherefore let those vainglorious worms confess the weakness of their reason, and the vanity of their efforts. Let them cease to deny the existence of Brama, or to prescribe limits to his power. Brama is, he is almighty, and he does not show himself to us more visibly in his dreadful scourges, than in his ineffable favours.
“But who has drawn down these scourges on this wretched country? Is it not thy injustice, greedy incredulous man? Thy gallantries and silly amours, worldly immodest woman! Thy excesses and shameful debaucheries, infamous man of pleasure! Thy hardheartedness for our monasteries, miser! Thy injustice, corrupt magistrate! Thy usuries, insatiable money-dealer! Thy effeminacy and irreligion, impious and sycophant courtier.
“And you, on whom this scourge is particularly fallen, women and maidens plunged into licentiousness; though we, renouncing the duties of our calling, should keep profound silence with regard to your irregularities, you carry about you a more importunate voice than ours: it accompanies you, and will everywhere rebuke you for your impure desires, your equivocal connections, your criminal conversations, such excessive care to please, so many artifices to engage, so much address to fix, and the impetuosity of your transports, and the fury of your jealousy. Why then do you delay to shake off the yoke of Cadabra, and return under the mild laws of Brama? But let us return to our subject. Well then I was saying that worldlings sit down heretically, and that for nine reasons; the first, etc.”
This discourse made very different impressions. Mangogul and the Sultana, who were the only persons that knew the secret of the ring, were convinced that the Bramin had as happily hit off the tattle of Toys by the assistance of religion, as Orcotomus by the light of reason. The court ladies and petits-maîtres declared the sermon seditious, and the preacher a visionary.

===Chinese===

物质的属性我们没有穷尽，它存在的新证据，只证明拿它驳我们的人的无知与轻信。在这块地基上，他们立体系、造假设、做实验：可是婆罗摩从他永恒的居所高处，哂笑他们虚妄的图谋。他叫狂妄的知识羞愧，玩具们把挡它们饶舌的无用的障碍，像玻璃一样撞得粉碎。所以，让那些虚荣的虫豸承认自己理性的孱弱、努力的徒劳吧。让他们别再否认婆罗摩的存在，别再给他的权能画界限。婆罗摩在，他全能；他在可怕的鞭子里显自己，不比在他妙不可言的恩宠里显自己更少。

“可是，是谁把这些鞭子引到这可怜的国度头上的？岂不是你的不义，贪婪无信的男人？你的调情和愚蠢的情史，世俗而无耻的女人！你的过度和可耻的纵欲，无耻的浪子！你对修道院的铁石心肠，吝啬鬼！你的不义，腐败的法官！你的重利盘剥，填不满的银钱贩子！你的柔靡与无神，不敬而谄佞的朝臣！

“还有你们，这鞭子落得格外重的人，陷在淫逸里的妇人和闺女；纵然我们背弃圣职，对你们的越轨守口如瓶，你们身上也带着一个比我们更缠人的声音：它跟着你们，到处数落你们不洁的欲念、暧昧的交往、罪愆的私语、那许多过分的讨人喜欢、那许多钩人的手段、那许多拴人的心机、你们狂热的失态、你们嫉妒的凶焰。那还等什么，快挣脱卡达布拉的轭，回到婆罗摩宽和的法度底下来！——罢了，回到正题。方才我说到，俗人入座坐成异端，有九层道理；第一层，等等。”

这篇讲道留下的印象大不相同。曼戈古尔同苏丹娜——唯一晓得戒指秘密的两个人——深信这婆罗门借宗教之力论玩具的闲话，论得同奥尔科托穆斯凭理性之光一样妥帖。宫里的太太和纨绔子们则断言，这布道是煽动，布道的是个妄人。

===Original===

The rest of the audience esteem’d him a prophet, shed tears, fell to prayers, and even flagellations, and did not change their courses of life.
The noise of this sermon spread to the very coffeehouses. A wit pronounced in a decisive tone, that the Bramin had but very superficially handled the subject, and that his discourse was but a cold insipid declamation: but in the opinion of the devout women and the enlightened, it was the most solid piece of eloquence that had been delivered in the temples these hundred years. And in mine, both the wit and the devout women were in the right.

===Chinese===

其余的会众尊他为先知，落泪，祷告，甚至行鞭笞，生活方式却分毫未改。

这番布道的声浪一直传到咖啡馆。一位才子以决断的口气说，婆罗门把题目处理得极浅，通篇不过一篇冷淡乏味的陈词；可依虔诚的太太和开明人士看，这是一百年来庙堂里发表过的最扎实的雄辩。依我看，才子和虔诚的太太都对。

## XVI The Muzzles / 口笼
===Original===

While the Bramins were making Brama speak, airing their Pagodas by processions, and exhorting the people to repentance; others were thinking how to reap benefit by the prattle of Toys.
Great cities swarm with persons, whom misery renders industrious.﻿—They neither rob nor pick pockets: but they are to pickpockets, what pickpockets are to gamblers. They know everything, they do everything. They go up and down, they insinuate themselves. They are found at court, in the city, at Westminster hall, at church, at the play, at ladies toilets, in coffeehouses, at balls, operas and in academies. They are anything that you would have them be. Do you sollicit a pension, they have the minister’s ear. Have you a lawsuit, they will solicit for you. Do you love gaming, they will make a party with you; good cheer, they keep a good kitchen; women, they will introduce you to Amina or Acaris. From which of the two would you please to purchase the distemper? Take your choice, they will undertake your cure. Their chief occupation is to find the ridicules of private persons, and to make advantage of the follies of the public. From them it is, that papers are distributed in the streets, at the gates of temples, at the playhouse doors, and other public places, by which you are advertised gratis, that such an one living at the Louvre, St. John’s, the temple, or the abbey, at such a sign, dupes mankind at home from nine in the morning to noon, and abroad the rest of the day.
Scarce had the Toys began to speak, when one of these sharpers fill’d the houses of Banza with a printed bill of this form and tenor.﻿—Advertisement to the ladies. And underneath in small Italic, by permission of my lord the great Seneschal, and with the approbation of the gentlemen of the royal academy of sciences. And lower down: The Sieur Eolipila of the royal academy of Banza, Member of the royal society of Monoémugi, of the imperial academy of Biafara, of the academy of the curious of Loango, of the society of Camur in Monomotapa, of the institute of Ereeco, and of the royal academies of Beleguanza and Angola; who has for many years given courses of baubles, with the applause of the court, the town, and the province; has, in favour of the fair sex, invented muzzles or portable gags, which deprive Toys of the use of speech, without obstructing their natural functions. They are neat and convenient.

===Chinese===

婆罗门们正忙着替婆罗摩传话、抬着宝塔游街薰香、劝百姓悔改，另一些人却在盘算怎样从玩具的闲话里收利。

大都会里挤满了被穷困逼得勤快起来的人。——他们不抬劫，也不扒包：可是同扒手相比，他们正如扒手同赌客相比。他们无所不知，无所不为。他们上蹿下跳，见缝就钻。宫廷里有他们，城里有他们，威斯敏斯特法庭有他们，教堂有他们，戏院有他们，太太的梳妆台边有他们，咖啡馆、舞会、歌剧、学院里都有他们。你要他们是什么，他们就是什么。你想讨一份年俸，他们同大臣说得上话。你有官司，他们替你去疏通。你爱赌，他们搭你的局；爱吃，他们养着一手好厨；爱女人，他们把你引见给阿米娜或阿卡丽丝。两位之中你高兴从哪位染上病，随你挑；治病他们也包。他们的头号营生，是搜刮私人的笑柄，再拿大众的愚蠢卖钱。沿街、庙门口、戏院门口和别的公众场所散发的那些传单，就出自他们之手——单上白告诉你，某某人，住在卢浮宫区、圣约翰区、圣殿区或修道院区，挂着什么幌子，每天上午九点到晌午在家哄人，其余时辰在外头哄人。

玩具们才开口不多时，这路人物中的一个，就把一张印就的招贴贴满了班扎全城，格式与条文如下。——敬告女上班。下面一行小斜体：蒙御前总管大臣恩准，并经王家科学院诸位先生审定。再下面：班扎王家科学院的厄奥利皮拉先生，莫诺埃穆吉王家学会、比亚法拉帝国学院、洛安戈好奇者学会、莫诺莫塔帕卡穆尔学会、埃雷科学会暨贝莱瓜扎与安哥拉王家诸学院会员；多年来开授小玩物课程，誉满宫廷、都城与外省；今为造福女界，发明口笼兼便携塞口器，能夺玩具之言而不碍其天然功用。制工精洁，使用便利。

===Original===

He has some of all sizes, of all prices, and for all ages: and he has had the honour to serve persons of the highest distinction with them.
There is nothing like being a member of a body of men. Be a work ever so ridiculous, it is puff’d into success. Thus it was, that Eolipila’s invention gain’d ground. People flock’d to his house. The gay ladies went thither in their own equipages, prudent women in hackney-coaches; the devouts sent their confessors or footmen, and nuns their doorkeepers. Everybody must have a muzzle, so that not one from the dutchess down to the cobler’s Joan, but had one either for the fashion or for reasons.
The Bramins, who had declared the prattle of Toys to be a divine punishment, and flatter’d themselves with a reformation of manners and other advantages from it, could not without horror see a machine, which eluded the vengeance of heaven and their hopes. Scarcely had they come down from their pulpits, but they mount again, thunder, roar, make the oracles to speak, and pronounce that a muzzle is an infernal machine, and that there is no salvation for those who shall use them. “Carnal women, quit your muzzles; submit,” cried they, “to the will of Brama. Permit the voice of your Toys to awaken that of your consciences, and blush not to acknowledge crimes, which you have not been ashamed to commit.”
But their exclamations were of little avail: the same fate attended muzzles, that had formerly attended the gowns without sleeves. The preachers were left to catch cold in the temples. All the women took gags, and quitted them not, either till they found them useless, or were tired of them.

===Chinese===

各尺寸、各价目、各年龄俱全：并曾荣幸地侍奉过多位贵妇名媛。

入会的身分就是值钱。再荒唐的东西，只要众人一吹，也就成了。于是厄奥利皮拉的发明渐渐吃开。人们成群往他宅里涌。轻快的太太们乘自家的车驾去，稳重的太太们雇街车去；虔诚的派忏悔师或听差去，修女派看门人去。人人必得一口笼，上自公爵夫人，下至补鞋匠家的琼姑娘，没有一个不备一口——或为时髦，或为缘故。

婆罗门们既已宣布玩具的聒噪是天谴，又指望借此整饬风俗、另收些好处，如今见一架机器既逃了上天的报复、又破了他们的指望，怎能不骇然。他们才下讲坛，又重新登坛，打雷，吼叫，请神谕发话，断言口笼乃地狱的机关，凡使用者绝无得救。“肉体的女儿们，摘下你们的口笼；顺从，”他们喊，“顺从婆罗摩的旨意。容你们玩具的声音唤醒你们良心的声音吧；去认你们犯下时不曾羞、如今也不必羞的罪吧。”

可他们的吶喊没有多大用处：口笼遭的命运，同当年无袖袍一样。布道的人们只好在庙堂里晾着嗓子。女人们人人上了口笼，再不离口——直到发觉没用，或用淢了为止。

## XVII The Two Devouts / 两位信女
===Original===

For some days past the Sultan left the Toys at rest. Important affairs, on which he was busy, suspended the effects of his ring. In this interval it was, that two women of Banza made diversion for the whole town.
They were professed devouts. They had managed their intrigues with all possible discretion, and were in full enjoyment of a reputation, which even the malice of those of their own class paid respect to. In the mosques nothing was talk’d of but their virtue. Mothers proposed them as patterns to their daughters, and husbands to their wives. The principal maxim of both was, that scandal is the greatest of all sins. This conformity of sentiments, but above all, the difficulty of edifying a clear-sighted and crafty neighbour at an easy expense, had got the better of the difference of their tempers, and they were very good friends.
Zelida received Sophia’s Bramin: and it was at Sophia’s house that Zelida confer’d with her director; and by a little self-examination, the one could hardly be ignorant of what concerned the other’s Toy: but the whimsical indiscretion of these Toys kept them both in cruel apprehensions. They held themselves as on the point of being unmask’d, and of losing that reputation of virtue, which had cost them fifteen years dissimulation and management, and which now embarassed them very much.
At some moments they would even forfeit their lives, especially Zelida, to be as much blasted as the greatest part of their acquaintance. “What will the world say? What will my husband do?﻿—What! that woman so reserved, so modest, so virtuous, that Zelida, like others, is but﻿—Alas! this thought distracts me!﻿—Yes, I wish I never had any reputation,” cried Zelida in a passion.
She was then with her female friend, who was making the same reflections, but without such violent commotion. Zelida’s last words made her smile. “Laugh, madam, without constraint. Burst out,” said Zelida, touched to the quick. “To be sure you have good cause.”
“I am as sensible of the impending danger, as you can be,” answered Sophia, with an air of indifference; “but how to shun it? For you will agree with me, that there is no likelyhood that your wish will be accomplish’d.”
“Contrive an expedient then,” replied Zelida: “Oh!” said Sophia, “I am tired of rummaging my brain, I can find none.

===Chinese===

有几天，苏丹让玩具们歇了。他手头有要紧的政务，把戒指的效验悬了起来。就在这个空当里，班扎的两个女人，给全城供了笑料。

她们是发过愿的信女。她们的私情料理得不能再谨慎，安享着一份连同行妇人的恶意都得敬让三分的名声。清真寺里，谈的净是她们的贤德。母亲拿她们给女儿作榜样，丈夫拿她们给太太作楷模。两人共同的第一信条是：丑闻是一切罪中最大的罪。这份见解的一致，尤其因为——要花小价钱骗过一个眼尖心刁的邻人实在不易——压倒了两人性情的差异，她们成了极好的朋友。

泽莉达（Zelida）家里接见索菲娅的婆罗门；泽莉达同她的神师密谈，也在索菲娅（Sophia）府上；只消稍稍自查一下，这一个对自己那件玩具的底细，就差不多瞒不过那一个：可这些玩具的古怪失口，把两人一齐吊在惶惶里。她们自觉随时就要被揭穿，就要丢掉那份贤德的名声——那名声费了她们十五年伪装与经营，如今又重重地拖累着她们。

有些时刻，她们情愿搭上性命——泽莉达尤其如此——只求同相识的大多数一样烂掉。“世人会怎么说？我丈夫会怎么做？——什么！那个女人，那样矜持、那样端谨、那样贤德，那个泽莉达，同旁人一样，不过——唉！这念头搅得我要疯了！——是，我恨不得从来就没有过什么名声，”泽莉达激动地喊。

她当时正在女友家里；女友也在转着同样的念头，只是没有这样翻江倒海。泽莉达末了那几句叫她笑了。“笑吧，夫人，别憋着。敞开笑，”泽莉达被刺到痛处，说。“您自然有十足的理由笑。”

“眼下的危险，我同您一样感觉得到，”索菲娅带答不理地答道；“可怎么躲？您总得同我一样承认，您那个愿望，没有实现的指望。”

“那就想个法子，”泽莉达回道。“哦！”索菲娅说，“我把脑子翻遍了，累了，一条也想不出。

===Original===

﻿—To bury one’s self in a country seat, is one way; but to abandon the pleasures of Banza, and renounce life, is what I will never do. I perceive that my Toy will never approve it.”
“What is to be done then?”
“What! to leave all to providence, and laugh, as I do, at what the world will say. I have tried all shifts to reconcile reputation with pleasures: but since it is decreed that we must renounce reputation, let us at least preserve pleasures. We were uniques: but now, my dear, we shall be like a hundred thousand others; do you look on this as a hard fate?”
“Yes, without doubt,” replied Zelida; “to me it seems hard to be like those, for whom I had put on a sovereign contempt. In order to avoid this mortification, methinks I would fly to the world’s end.”
“Set out, my dear,” continued Sophia; “for my part, I stay﻿—But apropos, I advise you to furnish yourself with some secret, to prevent your Toy from blabbing on the road.”
“Indeed,” replied Zelida, “pleasantry here has a very ill grace, and your intrepidity﻿—”
“You are mistaken, Zelida, there is not a grain of intrepidity in my proceeding. To let things go their own way, when we cannot stop them, is resignation. I see that I am to be dishonoured: well then, dishonour for dishonour, I shall spare myself as much of the uneasiness as I can.”
“Dishonour’d!” replied Zelida, bursting into tears. “Dishonour’d! What a shock! I cannot bear it.﻿—Oh! accursed Bonza, ’tis thou that hast ruin’d me. I loved my husband, I was born virtuous; I should have loved him still, if thou hadst not made a wicked use of thy ministry and my confidence. Dishonour’d, dear Sophia!﻿—”
She had not power to make an end. Sobs intercepted her words, and she fell on the carpet, quite in despair. As soon as Zelida recovered her speech, she cried out in a lamentable tone: “Alas! my dear Sophia, I shall die﻿—I must die. No, I shall never survive my reputation.”
“But Zelida, my dear Zelida, do not be in a hurry to die: perhaps﻿—” said Sophia.
“No perhaps shall stop me, I must die.”
“But perhaps one might﻿—”
“One might do nothing, I tell you﻿—But speak, my dear, what might one do?”
“Perhaps one might hinder a Toy from talking.”
“Ah! Sophia, you endeavour to comfort me by false hopes, you deceive me.”
“No, no, I do not deceive you; only hear me, instead of distracting yourself like a mad creature.

===Chinese===

——躲到乡下庄子去是一条路；可要舍了班扎的乐子、辞了人世，我永远不干。我看我的玩具也永远不会赞成。”

“那怎么办呢？”

“怎么办！把一切都交给天意，世人爱说什么，随他们去，像我这样一笑置之。为了把名声同乐子捏在一处，什么招我没试过：可既已注定要舍名声，那至少保住乐子。我们本是独一份儿：如今呢，亲爱的，咱们要同十万旁人一样了；您觉得这是苦命么？”

“是，毫无疑问，”泽莉达回道；“在我看来，同那些人一样——那些我一直端着十二分轻蔑的人——就是苦。为躲这一层屈辱，我想我肯逃到天边去。”

“那就动身吧，亲爱的，”索菲娅接着说；“我呢，我留下——对了，顺便一句，我劝您随身备一个秘方，免得您的玩具在半路上漏风。”

“说真的，”泽莉达回道，“这时候打趣很不合适，您这份胆量——”

“您弄错了，泽莉达，我行事里没有一粒胆量。拦不住的事就随它去，这叫顺命。我明白我要名声扫地了：那么，扫就扫，我能省下几分不安，就省几分。”

“扫地！”泽莉达迸出眼泪。“扫地！多大的打击！我受不了。——哦！该咒的和尚，毁我的是你。我爱过我丈夫，我生来端谨；若不是你拿你的圣职和我的信任行了恶，我到今天还爱着他。名声扫地了，亲爱的索菲娅！——”

她没有力气说完。呜咽堵住了话，她瘫在地毯上，绝望到底。泽莉达一缓过声来，便哀声道：“唉！亲爱的索菲娅，我要死了——我必得死。不，名声一去，我活不成。”

“可是泽莉达，亲爱的泽莉达，别急着死：也许——”索菲娅说。

“什么也许也拦不住我，我必得死。”

“可也许能有一个法子——”

“一个法子也没有，我告诉您——不过说吧，亲爱的，能有什么法子？”

“也许能有法子叫玩具开不了口。”

“啊！索菲娅，您拿空欢喜安慰我，您哄我。”

“不，不，我不哄您；只管听我说，别像疯子似的干着急。

===Original===

I have heard talk of Frenicol, Eolipila, gags and muzzles.”
“Pray, what connection is there between Frenicol, Eolipila, muzzles, and the danger which threatens us? What business has my Toyman here, and what is a muzzle?”
“It is this, my dear. A muzzle is a machine invented by Frenicol, approved by the academy, and improved by Eolipila, who claims the honour of the invention.”
“But pray, this machine invented by Frenicol, approved by the academy, and improved by that silly fellow Eolipila.”
“Oh! you are of a vivacity that surpasses imagination. Well then, this machine being applied to a Toy, renders it discreet in spite of its teeth.”
“Can this be true, my dear?”
“It is so said.”
“We must know it,” replied Zelida, “and immediately too.”
She rang, one of her women appeared, and she sent for Frenicol. “Why not for Eolipila?” said Sophia. “Frenicol is less taken notice of,” answered Zelida.
The Toyman came away with the messenger. “Ah! Frenicol, are you there,” said Zelida, “you are welcome. Make haste, my friend, to extricate two women out of cruel anxiety.”
“What is the business, ladies? Would you please to have some rare Toys?”
“No, we have two already, and we would willingly﻿—”
“Part with them, I suppose. Pray, ladies, let me see them, I will take them, or we will make an exchange.”
“You are mistaken, Mr. Frenicol, we have nothing to truck.”
“Well, I understand you, you have some earrings, which you would desire to lose, so as that your husbands may find them in my shop.”
“Not that neither; pray, Sophia, inform him of the matter.”
“Frenicol,” continued Sophia, “we want two﻿—What, don’t you understand?”
“No, madam: how would you have me understand, when you say nothing?”
“ ’Tis,” said Sophia, “because when a woman is modest, it gives her pain to speak plain on certain things.”
“But yet,” replied Frenicol, “she must speak plain. I am a Toyman, not a Conjuror.”
“You must guess however.”
“Faith, ladies, the more I look on ye, the less I comprehend ye. When a lady is young, rich, and pretty as you are, she is not reduced to artifice: moreover, I declare sincerely, that I sell them no longer. I have left the dealing in those baubles to young beginners in the trade.”
Our devouts found the Toyman’s mistake so ridiculous, that they both burst out into a violent fit of laughter, which disconcerted him. “Permit me, ladies, to make my bow, and withdraw.

===Chinese===

我听人说起过弗雷尼科尔、厄奥利皮拉、塞口器和口笼。”

“请问，弗雷尼科尔、厄奥利皮拉、口笼，同威胁咱们的危险有什么相干？我的玩具商跑这儿来做什么，口笼又是什么？”

“是这样，亲爱的。口笼是一架机器，弗雷尼科尔（Frenicol）发明，学院审定，厄奥利皮拉改良——改良的那位还抢发明的功劳呢。”

“请问，这架弗雷尼科尔发明、学院审定、蠢货厄奥利皮拉改良的机器——”

“哦！您的火气大得超乎想象。好，这架机器安到一只玩具上，它就满牙齿也规矩了。”

“这是真的么，亲爱的？”

“外头是这么说。”

“那必须弄清楚，”泽莉达回道，“而且立刻。”

她摇铃，一个女仆进来，她打发人去请弗雷尼科尔。“为什么不请厄奥利皮拉？”索菲娅说。“弗雷尼科尔惹眼少些，”泽莉达答。

玩具商跟着来人到了。“啊！弗雷尼科尔，您来了，”泽莉达说，“欢迎欢迎。快，朋友，把两个女人从水深火热里捞出来。”

“什么事，太太们？想要几件稀罕的玩具么？”

“不，我们已有两件，我们倒情愿——”

“出手，我猜是。请拿出来，太太们，我收下，或者咱们换个。”

“您误会了，弗雷尼科尔先生，我们没什么可换的。”

“哦，我懂了，您有副耳环想‘丢’，好叫您丈夫在我铺子里找到。”

“也不是；索菲娅，请您对他说吧。”

“弗雷尼科尔，”索菲娅接着说，“我们要两件——怎么，您还不懂？”

“不懂，夫人：您一言不发，叫我怎么懂？”

“是这么回事，”索菲娅说，“一个端谨的女人，有些事上要她把话说明白，是叫她疼的。”

“可再疼，”弗雷尼科尔回道，“也得说明白。我是玩具商，不是术士。”

“可您得猜。”

“真的，太太们，我越看你们越糊涂。一位太太，像您二位这样年轻、有钱、漂亮，是沦不到使手段的；再说，我诚心声明，那种小玩物我不卖了。那路买卖，我留给这行里的新学徒了。”

两位信女觉得玩具商的误会滑稽透顶，双双爆出一阵大笑，把他笑得发懵。“容我告退吧，太太们，让我行个礼走人。

===Original===

You might well have spared yourselves the trouble of sending for me three miles off, to divert yourselves at my cost.”
“Stop, stop, friend,” said Zelida, still laughing on. “That was not our intention. But by your misapprehending us, such ridiculous notions came into your head.”
“ ’Tis in your power, ladies, to put juster into it. What is the business?”
“Oh! Mr. Frenicol, let me laugh at ease, before I answer you.”
Zelida laugh’d till she panted for breath. The Toyman thought within himself that she had the vapors, or was out of her senses, and had patience. At length Zelida ceased.
“Well,” said she, “the business relates to our Toys, our own Toys, do you understand me, Mr. Frenicol? To be sure you cannot be ignorant, that for some time past several Toys have prattled like magpies: now we would be very glad that ours would not follow this bad example.”
“Ah! now I take it, that is to say,” replied Frenicol, “that you want a muzzle each.”
“Very right, you have hit it off truly. I have been told that Mr. Frenicol was no fool.”
“Madam, your goodness is very great. As to what you require, I have of all sorts, I go this moment to bring you some.”
Frenicol went accordingly: in the meanwhile Zelida embraced her friend, and thank’d her for her expedient: and I, says the African author, went to take a nap, waiting his return.

===Chinese===

打发人跑三里地把我叫来，拿我开心，这又何必。”

“站住，站住，朋友，”泽莉达还笑着。“我们本没有那个意思。倒是您这一误会，闹出这么多可笑的念头。”

“太太们要装得准些也容易。到底是什么事？”

“哦！弗雷尼科尔先生，容我笑够了再回您。”

泽莉达笑得直喘。玩具商心里想，她不是气郁症发了，就是疯了，只好耐着。泽莉达总算笑完了。

“好，”她说，“这事同咱们的玩具有关，咱们自己的玩具，您明白了么，弗雷尼科尔先生？想必您不至于不知道，近来有些个玩具，叨叨得像喜鹊：咱们呢，巴不得自己那两只别学这个坏样。”

“啊，这我懂了，就是说，”弗雷尼科尔回道，“您一人要一口笼。”

“一点不错，您真猜着了。早听人说，弗雷尼科尔先生不是傻子。”

“夫人，您过奖。您要的东西，样样有，我这就去取几种来。”

弗雷尼科尔去了；泽莉达趁这工夫抱住女友，谢她的妙计；而我呢——非洲作者说——也去打个盹，等他回来。

## XVIII The Toyman’s Return / 玩具商回来了
===Original===

The Toyman returned, and presented the ladies with two muzzles of the best sort.﻿—“Ah! mercy!” cried Zelida. “What enormous muzzles are these! And who are the unhappy women, whom these will fit? This is an ell long. Indeed, friend, you must certainly have taken measure by the Sultan’s mare.”
“Yes,” said Sophia indolently, after having measured them with her fingers, “you are in the right; and there is but the Sultan’s mare and old Rimosa, for whom these can do.”
“I protest, ladies, that they are of the common size; and that Zelmaida, Zyrphila, Amiana, and a hundred other ladies, have of the same sort.”
“That is impossible,” said Zelida.
“ ’Tis actually so however,” replied Frenicol: “but they all said the same thing as ye have: and as they have, so may you undeceive yourselves by trying them on.”
“Mr. Frenicol may say what he will; but he shall never persuade me that this will fit me,” said Zelida, “nor this me,” said Sophia. “Let him show us others, if he has any.”
Frenicol, who had often experienced that women are not to be converted on that article, show’d them muzzles for the age of thirteen. “Well,” cried both at the same time, “these are such as we want.”
“I wish they may prove so,” whisper’d Frenicol aside.
“How do you sell them?” said Zelida.
“But ten ducats a piece, madam.”
“Ten ducats, you forget yourself, Frenicol.”
“Madam, that is the price in conscience.”
“You make us pay for the novelty.”
“I protest, ladies, ’tis but two sixpences for a shilling.”
“I must own they are neatly made, but ten ducats are a great deal of money.”
“I’ll abate nothing.”
“We will go to Eolipila’s.”
“You may, ladies: but there are workmen and workmen, muzzles and muzzles.” Frenicol held firm, and Zelida came to. She paid for the two muzzles, and the Toyman went back, fully persuaded that they would be too little for them, and would soon be returned on his hands for a fourth part of the price he received for them. He was mistaken. Mangogul not happening to come within reach of turning his ring on those two women, their Toys were not seized with the humor of talking louder than ordinary; and happily for them: for Zelida having tried her muzzle, found it too little by one half. However, she did not part with it, fancying that it would be pretty near as inconvenient to change it, as not to make use of it at all.

===Chinese===

玩具商回来了，呈给两位太太两枚头等口笼。——“啊！天哪！”泽莉达叫道。“这口笼也大得吓人！哪位苦命女人戴得上？足有一厄尔长。说真的，朋友，您准是照苏丹家的母马量的尺寸。”

“是呢，”索菲娅拿手指量了量，懒懒地说，“您说得对；这尺寸，除了苏丹家的母马，就数老莉莫莎了。”

“我担保，太太们，这是寻常尺码；泽尔迈达、泽尔菲拉、阿米安娜，还有一百位太太，用的都是这一号。”

“不可能。”泽莉达说。

“可事实如此，”弗雷尼科尔回道：“不过她们起先也同二位一样说；她们怎样自己醒的，二位一试也就怎样。”

“弗雷尼科尔先生随怎么说，也休想叫我相信这枚我戴得下，”泽莉达说。“这枚我也戴不下，”索菲娅说。“有别的就再拿来看看。”

弗雷尼科尔屡试不爽，深知女人在这一条上是劝不转的，便拿出十三岁用的口笼来。“喏，”两人同时叫道，“这才正是我们要的。”

“但愿真是，”弗雷尼科尔低声自语。

“怎么卖？”泽莉达说。

“一枚才十杜卡多，夫人。”

“十杜卡多，你昏头了，弗雷尼科尔。”

“夫人，凭良心，这就是实价。”

“你这是拿新奇货宰我们。”

“我担保，太太们，这是一先令当两枚六便士使，只赚工钱。”

“我认账，做工是精；可十杜卡多到底是一大笔钱。”

“一个子儿不让。”

“我们上厄奥利皮拉家去。”

“随二位，太太们：不过工匠同工匠，口笼同口笼，可有高下。”弗雷尼科尔咬定不放，泽莉达松了口。她付了两枚口笼的钱；玩具商回去，满心笃定：这尺寸对她们一定太小，过不多久就得退回他手上，折四分之一价收。他猜错了。曼戈古尔碰巧没有转到能把戒指照上这两个女人的地方，她们的玩具没染上高声说话的瘾；这也算她们走运：因为泽莉达把口笼一试，发现小了一半。可她也没有退货，心想：换一枚同不用，横竖一样不方便。

===Original===

These circumstances came to light from one of her women, who told them in confidence to her lover, who related them in confidence to others, who spread them all over Banza under the seal of secrecy. Nor was Frenicol silent: the adventure of the devouts became public, and for sometime afforded employment to the calumniators of Congo.
Zelida became inconsolable upon it. This woman, more to be pitied than blamed, conceived an aversion for her Bramin, quitted her husband, and shut herself up in a convent. As for Sophia, she threw off the mask, despised censures, patch’d and painted, frequented public places, and had adventures.

===Chinese===

这些情形，是从她的一个女仆嘴里漏出来的：女仆悄悄告诉了情人，情人悄悄告诉了旁人，旁人贴着“守密”的封条，传遍了班扎。弗雷尼科尔也没闲着：信女的艳闻闹得尽人皆知，一时成了刚果造谣家们的营生。

泽莉达为此一蹶不振。这女人可悯多于可责：她对婆罗门起了厌心，离开丈夫，把自己关进了修院。至于索菲娅，她索性撕下面具，把物议踩在脚下，贴面，抹粉，出入公共场所，有了艳遇。

## XIX Seventh Trial of the Ring / 戒指的第七次试验
===Original===

Though the female cits of Banza doubted whether Toys of their rank would be honoured with the gift of speech; yet they all furnish’d themselves with muzzles. In Banza muzzles became as common, as a general court mourning in this country.
Here the African author observes with astonishment, that neither the lowness of the price, nor the commonness of muzzles put them out of fashion in the Seraglio. “This once,” says he, “utility got the better of prejudice.” So trite a reflection was not worth the pains of repeating: but to me it seems as if it was the defect of all the ancient authors of Congo, to fall into repetitions; whether they thereby hoped to give a greater air of truth and facility to their productions; or that they were far from having as much fecundity of invention as their admirers ascribe to them.
However that be, one day Mangogul, walking in his gardens, attended by his whole court, took the maggot to level his ring at Zelais. She was handsome, and suspected of several adventures: yet her Toy did no more than stammer, and utter’d only some few mangled words, which had no meaning, and which the Blasters interpreted as they thought fit.﻿—“Pshuh!” says the Sultan, “here is a Toy that has a vast impediment of speech. Most certainly there must be somewhat that impedes its respiration.” Wherefore he applied his ring more intensely. The Toy made a second effort to speak; and in some measure surmounting the obstacle, that stop’d its mouth, these words were heard very distinctly. “Alas! alas!﻿—I am﻿—I am﻿—I am stifled. I can no more.﻿—Alas! alas! I am stifled.”
Immediately Zelais found herself going: she grew pale, her neck swell’d, and she fell, with her eyes shut and mouth half open, between the arms of those who stood around her.
Anywhere else Zelais would have been speedily relieved. ’Twas only debarrassing her of her muzzle, and allowing her Toy a freedom of respiration: but how to lend the helping hand in Mangogul’s presence. “Quick, quick, physicians,” cried the Sultan, “Zelais is dying.”
Some pages ran to the palace, and returned, with the doctors gravely marching after them. Orcotomus was at their head. Some gave their opinion for bleeding, others for the kermes mineral; but the penetrating Orcotomus ordered Zelais to be carried to a neighbouring closet, examined her, and cut the braces of her harness.

===Chinese===

班扎的市民太太们虽疑心自己这等级的玩具，未必叨得说话的天恩，却人人置办了口笼。口笼在班扎流行起来，就像咱们这儿宫廷大丧一样普遍。

在此，非洲作者惊讶地议论道：口笼价钱之贱、销路之广，竟没有叫它在后宫失宠。“这一回，”他说，“实用压倒了成见。”这样陈腐的感想，本不值得重抄一遍；可我看来，刚果的古典作家们仿佛个个都有这毛病，好犯重复——不知他们是想借此给自己的作品添几分可信与流畅呢，还是他们的创造力，远不及其崇拜者吹的那样茂盛。

无论怎样，有一天，曼戈古尔带着满朝扈从在御花园散步，忽然起意，把戒指对准了泽莱丝（Zelais）。她漂亮，艳遇的嫌疑也有几桩：可她的玩具只结巴了几声，吐出几个残缺的字，不成意思，吹鼓手们只管随意解释。“嗐！”苏丹说，“这只玩具口吃得不轻。必定有什么东西碍着它的呼吸。”于是他把戒指催得更紧。玩具第二次挣扎着要说话，多少翻过了堵嘴的障碍，清清楚楚吐出这样几句：“唉！唉！——我——我——我闷死了。受不了啦。——唉！唉！我闷死了。”

泽莱丝登时觉得不行了：她脸白了，脖颈涨起来，眼睛闭着、嘴半张着，倒在身边人们的怀里。

在别处，泽莱丝立刻就得救了。只消替她摘下口笼，还她玩具一口自由的气：可当着曼戈古尔，谁敢伸这只援手。“快，快，传医官，”苏丹喊，“泽莱丝要死了。”

几个侍童奔进宫殿，领着大夫们鱼贯而出，个个走得凝重，为首的是奥尔科托穆斯。有的主张放血，有的主张锑剂；唯有眼利的奥尔科托穆斯，命人把泽莱丝抬进旁边一间小屋，亲自查验，割断了她口笼的带子。

===Original===

This muzzled Toy was one of those, which he boasted to have seen in the paroxysm.
However, the bloating was excessive, and Zelais would have continued to suffer, had not the Sultan had pity on her condition. He turn’d off his ring, the fluids return’d to their equilibrium. Zelais came to herself, and Orcotomus assumed the honour of this miraculous cure.
Zelais’s accident and her physician’s indiscretion, made the muzzles lose much of their credit. Orcotomus, without any regard to Eolipila’s interest, proposed to himself to raise his own fortune on the ruins of the others; advertised himself as a patentee physician of Toys that had caught cold: and some of his bills are to be seen at this day in the by-streets. He began by getting money, and ended by falling into contempt. The Sultan took pleasure in lowering the presumption of the empiric. Did Orcotomus boast of silencing a Toy that had never utter’d a word? Mangogul had the cruelty to make it speak. People went so far as to remark, that any Toy that was tired of silence, need only have two or three visits from Orcotomus. In a little time he was rank’d with Eolipila in the class of quacks; and they will both continue therein, until it shall please Brama to take them out.
Shame was preferred to the apoplexy. “The one kills,” said the women, “the other does not.” Wherefore they renounced their muzzles; let their Toys talk on; and nobody died thereby.

===Chinese===

这只戴笼的玩具，正是他自夸在阵发里见过那一路。

无奈胀得太凶，若不是苏丹怜惜她的处境，泽莱丝还得受下去。他把戒指转开，体液回到平衡。泽莱丝缓过来了，奥尔科托穆斯便揽下了这场妙手回春的光荣。

泽莱丝的事故，加上她医师的失口，叫口笼的信誉折损大半。奥尔科托穆斯全不 顾厄奥利皮拉的利益，一心踩着旁人的废墟起自己的家业；挂出了“玩具着凉专科、专利医师”的招牌，至今在僻巷里还见得到他几张告贴。他先赚了钱，末了跌进轻蔑。苏丹以杀这江湖郎中的威风为乐。奥尔科托穆斯不是夸口镇住过一只从没出过声的玩具么？曼戈古尔残忍地偏叫它开口。人们甚至传说：哪只玩具闷得受不住了，只消奥尔科托穆斯上门两三回。不多时，他就同厄奥利皮拉并了肩，归入国医一流；两位且要在那里待下去，直到婆罗摩高兴把他们请出来。

两害相权，太太们拣了丢脸，撇了中风。“一个要命，”她们说，“一个不要。”于是她们扔开口笼，由玩具说去；也没什么人为此送命。

## XX Eighth Trial of the Ring / 戒指的第八次试验
===Original===

There was a time, as we have seen, when the women dreaded the prating of their Toys, were stifled, and died away; but another succeeded, when they set themselves above this dread, threw away their muzzles, and had nothing beyond the vapors.
The favorite, among her attendants, had a very singular young lady. Her humour was charming, though uneven. She changed countenance ten times a day; but whatever face she put on, was pleasing. Unique in her melancholy, as well as in her gaiety, there slip’d from her, in her most extravagant moments, things of exquisite sense; and in her fits of sadness she uttered very diverting extravagances.
Mirzoza was so used to Callirhoe (for that is the name of this young mad girl) that she could hardly be without her. One time that the Sultan complain’d to the favorite of somewhat restless and cold, which he remark’d in her; “Prince,” said she, embarrassed at his reproach, “without my three beasts, my nightingale, my lapdog and Callirhoe, I am good for nothing; and you see that I have not the last.”
“And why is she not here?” said Mangogul. “I can’t tell,” answered Mirzoza; “but I remember, that some months ago she told me, that if Mazul made the campaign, she could not avoid having the vapors; and Mazul set out yesterday.”
“I easily excuse her,” said the Sultan: “for I look on her vapors to be well grounded. But upon what account do a hundred other women take it into their heads to be vapor’d, who have young husbands, and besides take care to be well provided with lovers?”
“Prince,” answered a courtier, “ ’tis a fashionable disease. It is genteel in a woman to have the vapors. Without a lover and the vapors, she knows nothing of the world: and there is not a citizen’s wife in Banza, who does not affect to have them.”
Mangogul smiled, and resolved to visit some of these vaporish women immediately. He went directly to Salica’s house, and found her in bed, with her neck quite bare, her eyes flaming, and her hair dishevel’d; and at her bed’s side the little stammering humpback’d physician Farfadi, telling her stories: while she was stretching out, now one arm, then another, yawning, sighing, putting her hand to her head, and exclaiming bitterly: “Alas I can’t bear it﻿—throw the windows open﻿—give me air﻿—I shall faint, I shall die.

===Chinese===

曾有一时，如我们所见，太太们怕玩具饶舌，怕到气闷、香消；可接着来了另一时，她们把这份怕踩到脚下，扔了口笼，只剩气郁症可害了。

宠妃的随员里，有位十分古怪的少女。她的脾性可人，只是阴晴不定。一天要换十回脸色；可无论换上哪一副，都讨人喜欢。她忧郁独一份，快活也独一份；最忘形的时刻，会漏出极精灵的话；伤感发作起来，又会说出非常好笑的痴语。

米尔佐扎对卡利罗厄（Callirhoe）——那便是这小疯姑娘的名字——惯到离不得。有一回苏丹向宠妃抱怨，说她身上有种不安和冷淡，是他看出来的。“君主，”她被这责备弄得很窘，“没了我的三只畜生——我的夜驾、我的哈巴狗、还有卡利罗厄——我就什么都干不成；您瞧，如今偏偏少了末了一只。”

“她怎么不在？”曼戈古尔说。“说不上来，”米尔佐扎答；“可我记得，几个月前她对我说过：马祖尔若是出征，她的气郁症就躲不掉；马祖尔昨天开拔了。”

“那我不怪她，”苏丹说：“她这气郁症，病得有根有据。可还有一百个女人，年轻的丈夫在家，外面又细心备足了情人，凭什么也要害气郁呢？”

“君主，”一位朝臣答道，“这是时新的病。女人害气郁，才算体面。没有情人又没有气郁症，就算不懂世故：如今班扎连市井太太，都装着要害这个呢。”

曼戈古尔笑了，决意立刻去访几位气郁太太。他径直到萨莉卡（Salica）家，见她躺在床上，脖颈袒露，两眼冒火，头发散乱；床边站着那个矮个儿、口吃、驼背的法尔法迪（Farfadi）医生，正讲故事给她听；她呢，一时伸出这条胳膊，一时那条，打着呵欠，叹着气，手按着头，惨声道：“唉，受不了了——把窗都推开——给我空气——我要晕了，我要死了。

===Original===

﻿—”
Mangogul took advantage of the moment that her affrighted women were assisting Farfadi in lightening the bed-cloths, to turn his ring on her, and instantly these words were heard: “Oh! how tired I am of this humor! behold, madam has taken into her noddle to have the vapors. This farce will last eight days at least, and may I die, if I know the cause: for after Farfadi’s efforts to ferret out this disease, I think it is in the wrong to continue.”
“Good,” said the Sultan turning off his ring, “I understand. This dame has the vapors in favor of her physician. Let us try elsewhere.”
He went from Salica’s house to that of Arsinoe, which is not far from it. On entering her chambers he heard loud bursts of laughter, and advanced, thinking to find her in company: but she was alone; and Mangogul was not much surprised. “A woman who gives herself the vapors,” says he, “gives them melancholy or merry, as is most convenient.” He turn’d his ring on her, and presently her Toy began to laugh unmercifully. From these immoderate laughing fits it made a sudden transition to ridiculous lamentations on the absence of Narces, whom it advised as a friend to hasten his return; and continued to sob, weep, groan, sigh, and be comfortless, as if it had buried all its relations.
The Sultan, who could hardly refrain from laughing aloud at so whimsical an affliction, turn’d off his ring and departed; leaving Arsinoe and her Toy to lament at leisure, and inferring the falsity of the proverb.

===Chinese===

——”

曼戈古尔趁她惊慌的女仆们帮着法尔法迪掀薄被褥的当口，把戒指转向她，立刻听见这样的话：“哦！这脾气我可淢透了！瞧，太太脑子里又起了害气郁的念头。这出戏至少要演八天；我要是知道缘故，叫我死：法尔法迪追这病根追了半天，我看这病再拖下去就是没道理了。”

“好，”苏丹把戒指转开，“我懂了。这位太太害气郁，是给她医师的面子。别处再试试。”

他出了萨莉卡家，去了阿尔西诺伊（Arsinoe）家，两家离得不远。一进闺房就听见一阵阵大笑；他往前走，只道她有客：却只见她独自一人；曼戈古尔也不怎么惊讶。“给自己害气郁症的女人，”他说，“看怎么方便，就害愁的或者喜的。”他把戒指转向她；她的玩具立刻大笑不止。笑了一阵狂的，又猛地转入可笑的恸哭，哭纳尔塞斯（Narces）不在，还以朋友的口气劝他快些回来；接着又抽噎、流泪、呻吟、长叹、寻死觅活，仿佛把满门亲眷都埋了。

苏丹险些当场笑出声，把戒指一转，走了；留阿尔西诺伊同她的玩具慢慢哭去，顺手给那句谚语断了个不实。

## XXI Ninth Trial of the Ring / 戒指的第九次试验
===Original===

Mangogul returning to his palace, pondering on the ridiculous airs which women give themselves, found himself, whether through absence of mind, or some blunder of his ring, under the portico of the sumptuous building, which Thelis has decorated with the rich spoils of her lovers. He embraced the opportunity, to interrogate her Toy.
Thelis was the wife of the Emir Sambuco, whose ancestors had reign’d in Guinea. Sambuco had acquired a high reputation in Congo, by five or six signal victories, which he had gain’d over Erguebzed’s enemies. As he was not a less able negotiator than a great captain, he had been employ’d in embassies of the greatest consequence, and executed his high trust with superior talents. On his return from Loango he saw Thelis, and was smitten. He was then upon the brink of fifty, and Thelis was not above twenty-five. She was rather agreeable than a beauty: the women said she was well enough, and the men thought her charming. Powerful matches sought her; but whether she had already fix’d her notions, or that there was too great a disproportion of fortune between her and her admirers, they were all rejected. Sambuco saw her, laid at her feet immense riches, a great name, laurels, and titles inferior to none but those of sovereigns, and obtain’d her.
Thelis was, or appear’d virtuous for six whole weeks after her marriage. But a Toy born voluptuous, seldom conquers itself; and a quinquagenarian husband, though otherwise ever so great a hero, is a madman, if he flatters himself with conquering such an enemy. Although Thelis mix’d prudence with her conduct, her first adventures were not unknown. This was sufficient for supposing afterwards, that she had others undisclosed: and Mangogul, desirous of full information, hastened to pass from the porch of her palace into her apartment.
It was then the middle of summer. The heat was excessive, and Thelis, after dining, had thrown herself on a couch, in a back closet adorned with glasses and paintings. She was asleep, her hand leaning on a collection of Persian tales, which had lull’d her to repose.
Mangogul view’d her some time, allowed that she had charms, and turn’d his ring on her. “I remember it as perfectly as if it had been yesterday: nine proofs of love in four hours. Ah! what moments! Zermounzaid is a divine man! He is not the old frozen Sambuco.

===Chinese===

曼戈古尔一面往宫里走，一面想着女人给自己摆的那副副可笑的架子，不知是心里出了神，还是戒指闹了个岔子，一抬头，已立在一座华厦的门廊下——那是忒利斯（Thelis）拿情人们的丰富战利品装点起来的。他趁此机会，审问她的玩具。

忒利斯是埃米尔桑布科（Sambuco）的妻子；桑布科的祖先曾在几内亚称王。桑布科在刚果享有盛名，靠的是对埃尔格贝泽德的敌人打下的五六场漂亮胜仗。他不但是名将，还是能手谈判家，屡次出任最要紧的使节，以过人的才具完成重任。从洛安戈回来，他见了忒利斯，一见倾心。他已年近五十，忒利斯不过二十五。她谈不上美人，却讨人喜欢：女人们说她还不错，男人们觉得她妩媚。有权势的人家纷纷来求；可不知是她主意早定，还是她同仰慕者之间家世悬殊太大，来的一概回绝。桑布科见了她，把泼天的财富、伟大的姓氏、桂冠，连同只逊于君王的显爵，一并摆在她脚下，把她娶到了手。

婚后整整六个星期，忒利斯是、或显得贞洁。可一只生来淫逸的玩具，难得管得住自己；一个五十岁的丈夫，别处再是英雄，若自诩降得住这样的对头，也是疯子。忒利斯行事虽掺着谨慎，她起初的几桩艳遇还是漏了风。这就尽够人推想：往后还有没漏的呢。曼戈古尔想探个全备，便赶紧从她府邸的门廊穿进她的闺房。

时值仲夏。酷热难当，忒利斯饭后倒在一张躺椅上，在一间缀着镜子和画的后屋里。她睡着了，手支在一部波斯故事集上——正是它哄她入睡的。

曼戈古尔端详了她一阵，认了她确实有她的迷人处，把戒指转向她。“我记得清清楚楚，仿佛昨天一般：四个钟头，九番恩爱。啊！那是何等的时刻！泽尔蒙扎伊德是位神仙！他可不是那冻僵的老桑布科。

===Original===

﻿—Dear Zermounzaid, I had been ignorant of true pleasures, real good: you alone made me sensible of them.”
Mangogul, desirous of learning the particulars of Thelis’s commerce with Zermounzaid, which the Toy kept from him, by dwelling on what affects a Toy the most feelingly, rubb’d the stone of his ring for some time against his waistcoat, and levell’d it, quite sparkling with light, at Thelis. Its influence soon reach’d her Toy, which being thereby better informed of what was required of it, re-assumed its discourse in a more historical strain.
“Sambuco commanded the Monoémugian army, and I followed him to the field. Zermounzaid served under him in quality of a colonel, and the general, who honoured him with his confidence, had put us under his escort. The zealous Zermounzaid did not abandon his post: he thought it too pleasing, to resign it: and the danger of losing it was the only one he fear’d during the whole campaign.
“While we were in winter quarters, I entertained a few new guests; Cacil, Jekia, Almamoun, Jasub, Selim, Manzora, Nereskim, all military men, who were commended by Zermounzaid, but were inferior to him. The credulous Sambuco relied for his wife’s virtue on herself, and on Zermounzaid’s care: and being entirely taken up with the immense business of the war, and the great operations which he was meditating for the glory of Congo, he never had the least suspicion either of the treachery of Zermounzaid, or the infidelity of Thelis.
“The war continued; the armies took the field, and we our litters. As they went a very slow pace, the main body of the army gain’d ground of us, and we found ourselves in the rear, which Zermounzaid commanded. This gallant youth, whom the sight of the greatest dangers had never made to swerve a foot from the path of glory, could not resist that of pleasure. He entrusted a subaltern with the care of watching the motions of the enemy, who harrassed us; and got up into our litter: but he was hardly there, when we heard a confused noise of arms and outcries. Zermounzaid, leaving his business half done, attempts to jump out: but he is level’d with the ground, and we become a prey to the conqueror.
“Thus I began by swallowing up the honor and services of an officer, who from his valour and merit might have expected the highest military employments, if he had never known his general’s wife.

===Chinese===

——亲爱的泽尔蒙扎伊德，我从前不懂什么叫真乐、真好处：是你一个人教我的。”

曼戈古尔急于探听忒利斯同泽尔蒙扎伊德交往的细情——玩具偏偏绕开这个，一味在玩具最动情的地方打转——他便把戒指的宝石朝马甲上蹭了好一阵，蹭得它火星四射，对准忒利斯。它的法力很快通到她的玩具；玩具明白了要它交代什么，重新开腔，换了史家的口吻。

““桑布科统率莫诺埃穆吉的军队，我随军出征。泽尔蒙扎伊德（Zermounzaid）在他麾下当上校；将军倚重他，把我们托给他护送。热心的泽尔蒙扎伊德不离岗位：那岗位太可意，舍不得交；整个战局里他唯一怕的危险，就是丢了这个差。

““我们扎冬营的时候，我又款待了几位新客：卡西尔、杰基亚、阿尔马蒙、贾苏布、塞利姆、曼佐拉、内雷斯金，全是军人，都是泽尔蒙扎伊德引荐的，却都不及他。轻信的桑布科，把太太的贞操托给了她自己，托给了泽尔蒙扎伊德的照拂；他一心忙着军务的大计、忙着为刚果的光荣筹划的大动作，对泽尔蒙扎伊德的背叛、对忒利斯的失节，从没起过一丝疑心。

““战事延续；大军开拔，我们也上了我们的担架。因为走得慢，本队把我们落下了，我们发现自己处在后卫——后卫正归泽尔蒙扎伊德指挥。这位英姿青年，最大的危险也不能叫他离开光荣之径半步，却挡不住快乐一望。他把瞭望敌军的差事交给一个下属——敌军正袭扰我们——自己爬进了我们的担架：可他刚上去，就听见一片兵器同喊杀的乱响。泽尔蒙扎伊德把事情办了一半，就要往外跳：却被掀翻在地，我们成了胜利者的猎物。

““我就是这样起头的：先吞掉一位军官的荣誉与功业——凭他的骁勇与才具，他本可指望军中最高的职位，倘若他从不认得他将军的太太。

===Original===

Upwards of three thousand men fell in this action; and therefore of so many good subjects have we robb’d the nation.”
Let anyone, if he can, imagine Mangogul’s surprise at this discourse. He had heard Zermounzaid’s funeral oration, and did not know him by these features. His father Erguebzed had regretted this officer: the newspapers, after lavishing the highest eulogies on his fine retreat, had attributed his defeat and death to the enemy’s superior numbers, which, as they said, were found to be six to one. All Congo had lamented a man, who had done his duty so well. His wife obtain’d a pension: his regiment was given to his eldest son, and an ecclesiastical preferment promised to his younger.
“What horror!” cried Mangogul, but softly. “A husband dishonor’d, the state betray’d, subjects sacrificed, crimes not only concealed, but even rewarded as virtues: and all that for a Toy.”
Thelis’s Toy, which had stopped to take breath, continued: “Thus am I abandoned to the enemy’s discretion. A regiment of dragoons was ready to fall upon us. Thelis seem’d quite frighted, and yet wish’d nothing more ardently: but the charms of the prey sowed discord among the plunderers. The scimiters were drawn, and thirty or forty men were massacred in the twinkling of an eye. The noise of this disorder reach’d the general officer. He ran thither, calmed the furious soldiers, and sequestrated us under a tent; where we had not time to know ourselves, when he came and demanded the price of his good services. ‘Woe to the vanquished,’ cried Thelis, falling backward on a bed: and the whole night was spent in feeling her misfortune.
“The next day we found ourselves on the banks of the Niger. A saick was waiting for us, and my mistress and I set out, in order to be presented to the emperor of Benin. In this twenty-four hours voyage the captain of the vessel offer’d himself to Thelis, and was accepted: and I found by experience, that the sea service is infinitely brisker than the land service.
“We saw the emperor of Benin. He was young, ardent, voluptuous. Thelis made a conquest of him: but those of her husband affrighted the monarch. He demanded peace: and the price, at which he purchased it, was but three provinces and my ransom.
“Different times, different fatigues.

===Chinese===

“这一仗，阵亡的超出三千人；这许多良民，就是我们这样从国家手里抢走的。”

谁有本事，谁去想象曼戈古尔听了这番话的惊骇吧。他听过泽尔蒙扎伊德的悼词，认不得这副面目的他。他父亲埃尔格贝泽德哀悼过这位军官：报纸先把他那场漂亮的退兵捧上了天，又把他的败绩同死，归给敌军的优势兵力——照报纸的说法，是一比六。全刚果都痛悼过一位恪尽职守的人。他的遗孀得了抚恤；团队给了他长子，还给幼子许了一个圣职。

“可怕！”曼戈古尔低声道。“丈夫蒙羞，国家被卖，臣民牺牲，罪恶不但没人知道，还被当作功德受赏：这一切，为了一只玩具。”

忒利斯的玩具喘了口气，接着讲：“我就这样被丢给了敌人发落。一个龙骑兵团眼看要扑上来。忒利斯显得魂飞魄散，实则求之不得；可猎物的美色在劫掠者中播下内江。弯刀出鞘，一眨眼三四十条人命没了。乱子惊动了统兵官。他跑来，压住暴怒的兵，把我们隔离在一顶帐篷下；我们还来不及认清自己，他已来讨他好意的报酬。‘败者该死，’忒利斯朝后倒在一张床上喊；整夜都在领受她的不幸。

““次日我们已在尼日尔河岸。一条帆船候着我们，我同女主人登程，预备去觐见贝宁皇帝。这二十四小时的航程里，船主向忒利斯自荐，被纳了；我这才凭经验得知：海上的差使，比陆上的爽利得多。

““我们见了贝宁皇帝。他年轻、热烈、淫逸。忒利斯把他降服了：可她丈夫的战绩吓坏了那位君主。他求和；讲和的代价，不过三行省外加把我赎回。

““不同的时节，不同的劳顿。

===Original===

Sambuco came to know, I can’t tell how, the reason of the misfortunes of the preceding campaign; and during this, he deposited me with a friend of his, a chief of the Bramins, on the frontiers. The holy man made but a weak defence: he was ensnared by the wiles of Thelis, and in less than six months devoured his immense income, three lakes and two forests.”
“Mercy,” cried Mangogul, “three lakes and two forests! What an appetite for a Toy!”
“ ’Tis a mere trifle,” resumed the Toy. “Peace was made, and Thelis accompanied her husband in his embassy to Monomotapa. She gamed, and very fairly lost a hundred thousand sequins in one day, which I won back again in an hour. A minister, whose master’s affairs did not fill up all his time, fell into my clutches; and in three or four months I eat him up a fine landed estate, together with his castle well furnish’d, a park, and equipage, with the little pied horses. A favor of four minutes duration, but well spun out, brought us in feasts, presents and jewels: and the blind or politic Sambuco did not disturb us.
“I shall not bring into the accompt,” added the Toy, “the marquisats, counties, titles, coats of arms, etc. which have been eclipsed at my appearance. Apply to my secretary, who will tell you what is become of them. I have close-pared the horns of the dominions of Biafara, and am in possession of an entire province of Beleguanza. Erguebzed made overtures to me a little before his death.” At these words Mangogul turn’d off his ring, and silenced this gulf: he respected his father’s memory, and would hear nothing that might tarnish the splendor of the great qualities, which he knew were in him.
Returning to the seraglio, he entertained the favorite with the vapored ladies, and with the trial of his ring on Thelis. “You admit this woman,” said he, “to your familiarity: but in all probability you do not know her as well as I.”
“I understand you, sir,” answered the Sultana. “Her Toy has perhaps been foolish enough to give you a narrative of her adventures with the general Micokof, the Emir Feridour, the senator Marsupha, and the great Bramin Ramanadanutio. But pray, who is ignorant, that she keeps young Alamir, and that old Sambuco, who speaks not a word, is as well apprized of it as you.”
“You have not hit the mark,” replied Mangogul. “I have compell’d her Toy to make a full discharge.”

===Chinese===

桑布科不知怎么打听到了前一役的不幸缘由；这一役里，他把我寄放在边境上一个朋友家——一位婆罗门首座。圣者的守御软弱无力：他被忒利斯的计网罩住，不出半年，吃尽了他偌大的岁入、三座湖和两片森林。”

“天哪，”曼戈古尔叫道，“三座湖和两片森林！好大的胃口，一只玩具！”

““这不算什么，”玩具接着说。“和议既成，忒利斯随丈夫出使莫诺莫塔帕。她赌钱，一天里干干净净输了十万西昆；不到一个钟头，又叫我赢了回来。一位主人家里闲得发慌的大臣，落进我的爪里；三四个月，我吃掉他一份上好的庄园，连带府邸家具、一座猎苑、车马，还有那几匹花斑小马。一场四分钟——却拖足了四分钟——的恩典，换来的是盛宴、礼物和珠宝：瞎眼的——或装瞎的——桑布科不来搅我们。

““我不打算记账的还有，”玩具补了一句，“那些在我面前黯然失色的侯国、伯国、爵衔、纹章等等。去问我的秘书，他会告诉你们它们的下落。比亚法拉的领地，角被我削得精光；贝莱瓜扎整整一省，握在我手里。埃尔格贝泽德临死前不久，还向我递过话呢。”听到这里，曼戈古尔把戒指一转，封了这张无底洞的口：他敬重亡父的纪念，不愿听任何可能玷污他明知父亲身上那份伟大品质的光辉的话。

回后宫的路上，他同宠妃聊起那几位气郁太太，聊起在忒利斯身上试的那一回。“你把这位太太引进你的亲近，”他说，“可十有八九，你不及我识得她。”

“我明白您的意思，殿下，”苏丹娜答道。“她的玩具许是又犯了傻，把她同米科科夫将军、费里杜尔埃米尔、元老马尔苏法、还有大婆罗门拉马纳达努蒂奥的账，全抖给您听了。可是请问，谁不知道她养着小阿拉米尔，连一言不发的老桑布科，都同您一样门儿清。”

“这回你可没猜中，”曼戈古尔回道。“我勒令她的玩具做了彻底的交代。”

===Original===

“Had it swallowed anything of yours,” said Mirzoza. “No,” said the Sultan, “but much belonging to my subjects, to the grandees of my empire, to the neighbouring potentates; as estates, provinces, castles, lakes, forests, diamonds, equipages, with the little pied horses.”
“Without reckoning their reputation and virtue, sir,” added Mirzoza. “I cannot tell what benefit you will reap by your ring; but the more you try it, the more odious my sex becomes to me: even those, whom I thought I justly held in some esteem, are not expected. They have thrown me me into such an humor, that I beg your highness will allow me to indulge it alone for some moments.” Mangogul, who knew that the favorite was an enemy to all constraint, kiss’d her right ear thrice, and retired.

===Chinese===

“它吞了您的东西不成？”米尔佐扎说。“没有，”苏丹说，“可吞了我臣民的、我帝国重臣的、邻邦君王的东西：庄园、行省、城堡、湖泊、森林、钻石、车马，连那几匹花斑小马。”

“还不算他们的名声和德行呢，殿下，”米尔佐扎补了一句。“您这戒指能给您创什么利，我说不上；可您越试，我越讨厌我的性别：连我自以为理当敬重几分的那几位，也指望不得。她们把我抛进了这样的心境，求殿下容我独自待它一会儿。”曼戈古尔深知宠妃最见不得勉强，在她右耳上亲了三下，退了出去。

## XXII A Sketch of Mangogul’s Moral Philosophy / 曼戈古尔道德哲学素描
===Original===

Mangogul, impatient to see the favorite again, slept little, arose earlier than usual, and was in her apartment before sun-rising. She had already rung the bell: a servant had just opened the curtains, and her women were preparing to dress her. The Sultan look’d narrowly around her, and seeing never a dog, he asked her the reason of this oddity. “I see,” answered Mirzoza, “that you think me singular in this article, but there is nothing in it.”
“I assure you,” replied the Sultan, “that I see dogs about all the women of my court, and you will oblige me in informing me why they have them, or why you have none. Most of them have several, and not one of them but lavishes such caresses on her own, as she seems not to bestow on her lover without some difficulty. How come these beasts to deserve the preference? What use is made of them?”
Mirzoza was puzzled at these questions: however, she made this answer. “To be sure, one keeps a dog as a parrot or a canary bird. It may be ridiculous to set one’s heart on these animals; but there is nothing wonderful in having them: they sometimes amuse, and never injure. If they are caressed, ’tis because such caresses are of no consequence. Besides, do you believe, prince, that a lover is satisfied with a kiss, such as a woman bestows on her pug-dog?”
“Doubtless, I believe it,” says the Sultan. “By Jupiter the man must be very nice, who would not be satisfied.”
