His devote friend, though intimately acquainted with all the transactions of heaven, knew as well as others, how a man should be made on earth: and the poor lad lost in a moment all the fruits of his good works. If anything consoled him, it was, that his secret was inviolably kept. One word would have rendered his disease incurable; but this word was not uttered, and Hilas linked in with some other pious women, whom he took, one after another, for the specific ordained by the oracle, and who did not break his enchantment, because they loved him only for what he was not.

===Chinese===

这事最糟的是，那傻姑娘把这事悄悄讲给了一位闺中密友；这位密友出于绝顶的稳重，只讲给了自己的三四个密友，她们又如法炮制，各讲给三四个：于是伊拉斯，两天之前还是全体女性的宠儿，转眼便受人白眼、遭人指点，被当作怪物看了。
“不幸的伊拉斯在本国被人骂倒，决意出远门，到异域去寻医问药。他独自上路，隐姓埋名来到阿比西尼亚皇帝的宫廷。年轻的外乡人被太太们一眼挑中，人人争抢：可伊拉斯审慎地避开了一切亲近——他确信追逐他的女人们在他身上讨不着好处，也就怕自己在她们身上讨不着对等的好处。可是请观察并叹服这一性别的眼力：‘这样年轻、这样俊美、又这样稳重的一个男人，’她们说，‘真是个奇物：’这几样品性集于一身，几乎叫她们疑到他的真短处；以致生怕多给了他一个完人该有的东西，反倒把他缺的那样也一并谢绝了。
“把这一国的舆图揣摩了一阵之后，伊拉斯结识了一位少妇；她出于某种没人说得清的反复，已从精致的风流跌进了最高的虔修。他渐渐浸润她的信任，附和她的见解，模仿她的功课，搀她进出神庙，又频频同她谈论尘世欢乐的虚妄，不知不觉竟唤回了她对那些欢乐的胃口，连同记忆。他们结伴上清真寺、听布道、探望病人，已然一月有余；他于是为一场彻底的痊愈做好了一切准备；可全是白费。他那位虔修的朋友，虽然对天上的事务了如指掌，男人在地上该长什么样子，却同旁人一样明白：可怜的青年顷刻之间，把善功的果实丢了个精光。若说还有什么可安慰的，便是他的秘密守得滴水不漏。一句话就能叫他的病不可救药；可这句话没有出口。于是伊拉斯又续上了另外几位虔诚的女子，一个接一个，把她们当作神谕指定的特效药；可她们谁也解不开他的魔咒，因为她们爱的只是他所不是的东西。

===Original===

The habit, they had acquired of spiritualizing all objects, was of no service to him. They required sense, but it was of that sort which springs from pleasure. Hilas complained that they did not love him. But their answer was, ‘pray, sir, are you ignorant, that people should know each other, before they love; nay, you must acknowledge, that, disgraced as you are, you are not lovely even when you are known.’
“ ‘Alas!’ said he retiring, ‘this pure love, so much talked of, is nowhere to be found; this delicacy of sentiments, upon which both sexes value themselves, is a mere chimaera. The oracle has deceived me, and my disease is for life.’
“In his way, he met some of those women, who allow no other commerce with man, but that of the heart, and who hate a forward spark like a toad. They so seriously recommended to him to let nothing gross and terrestrial enter into his views, that he conceived great hopes of his cure. He complied heartily, and was quite astonished, after the amorous conversations, which they held with him, that he still remained as he was. ‘I must certainly be cured,’ said he to himself, ‘but perhaps otherwise than by words:’ and he sought an occasion of placing himself according to the intentions of the oracle. Thus it soon offered. A young female platonic, who was excessively fond of walking, led him into a lonesome wood and when they had penetrated far from the reach of any impertinent eye, the fair one was seized with a fainting fit. Hilas threw himself on her, and neglected nothing in his power to relieve her; but all his endeavours were fruitless: of which she soon became as sensible as himself.
“ ‘Ah! Sir,’ cried she, disengaging herself from his arms, ‘what sort of man are you? I shall be very cautious of ever venturing thus into lonesome places, where I have been taken so ill, and may die a hundred times for want of help.’
“Others knew his condition, pitied him, protested to him, that the tenderness they had conceived for him, should not change; and never saw him more.
“The miserable Hilas, with his graceful figure, and the most refined sentiments, gave great dissatisfaction to many ladies.”
“Then he was a fool,” interrupted the Sultan. “Why did he not address some of the vestals, of which our monasteries are full? They would be charmed with him, and he would infallibly receive his cure through a grate.”

===Chinese===

她们养成的把万物灵性化的习惯，于他毫无补益。她们要的是感觉，不过是从快乐里生出的那一种。伊拉斯抱怨她们不爱他。她们却答道：‘请问，先生，您不知道人先该相知，然后相爱么？不但如此，您还得承认，您既已这般见辱，即便被人相知了，也不可爱。’
“‘唉！’他退下时叹道，‘人们津津乐道的纯洁之爱，无处可寻；两性各自夸耀的情感细腻，纯粹是个幻影。神谕骗了我，我的病是终身之疾了。’
“一路上，他又遇到那种只许男人同心交往、把轻薄的登徒子恨得像癫蛤蟆的女人。她们恳切地叮嘱他，眼里容不得半点粗鄙尘俗的东西，他听了，痊愈的希望大增。他诚心照办；可同她们进行过那一番番缠绵的谈话之后，发现自己依然故我，不由大为诧异。‘想必是痊愈了，’他自忖，‘只是治好的路数不在言语上罢：’他便寻机按神谕的意图行事。机会很快来了。一位酷爱散步的年轻的柏拉图派女子，把他领进一座幽林；待两人钻到任何不怀好意的眼力都够不着的地方，美人儿晕了过去。伊拉斯扑到她身上，使出浑身解数救她；可一切努力皆是徒劳：这一点，她很快同他一样明了了。
“‘啊！先生，’她挣脱他的怀抱喊道，‘您算个什么男人？往后我可要加倍小心，再不敢贸然到这种僻静地方来了：我在这里病得多沉，说不定一百回都要没命，没人搭救。’
“另一些女子知道了他的底细，怜悯他，向他担保，她们对他怀下的柔情绝不改变；从此再没见过他。
“可怜的伊拉斯，空有一副风流仪表、一身最细腻的情操，却叫许多夫人大失所望。”
“那他是个傻子，”苏丹插话道。“为何不去找我们修道院里多的是的修女？她们准会被他迷住，隔着栅栏他准能领到他的痊愈。”

===Original===

“Prince,” replied Selim, “chronicles assure us, that he tried that method, and found by experience, that the sex of all conditions do not care to love to their certain loss.”
“If that be the case,” said the Sultan, “I take his distemper to be incurable.”
“So did he himself as well as your highness,” continued Selim; “and tired out with unsuccessful trials, he plunged himself into solitude, on the word of an infinite number of women, who had expresly declared to him, that he was useless in society.
“He had already been several days rambling in a desert, when he heard some sighs issuing from a lonely place. He listened, the sighs began again, he drew near, and saw a young maiden, fair as the morn, her head leaning on her hand, her eyes bathed in tears, and the rest of her body in a pensive and mournful posture.
“ ‘What seek you here, madam?’ said he to her. ‘And are these deserts made for you?’
“ ‘Yes,’ replied she in a melancholy strain: ‘I can at least afflict myself here quite at my ease.’
“ ‘And what is the cause of your affliction?’
“ ‘Alas!’
“ ‘Speak, madam, what is the cause?’
“ ‘Nothing.’
“ ‘How, nothing?’
“ ‘No, nothing at all, I say, is the cause of my grief: two years ago I had the misfortune to offend a Pagoda, who deprived me of my all: and it was so small a matter, that in so doing, she gave no great proof of her power. Since that time, all the men shun me, and will shun me, said the Pagoda, until I meet with one, who knowing my misfortune, will cleave to me, and love me in the state, in which I am.’
“ ‘What do I hear!’ cried Hilas. ‘This wretch, whom you behold at your feet, has nothing, and that is his distemper likewise. Some time since he had the misfortune to offend a Pagoda, who deprived him of all that he had, and without vanity it was somewhat. From that time all the women shun him, and will shun him, said the Pagoda, until he meets with one, who, knowing his misfortune, will cleave to him, and love him in the state, in which he is.’
“ ‘Is this possible?’ says the young maiden.
“ ‘Is what you have told me true?’ says Hilas.
“ ‘See,’ answered the maiden.
“ ‘See,’ answered Hilas.
“They both convinced each other beyond all doubt, that they were two objects of the celestial wrath. Their common misfortune united them. Iphis, this was the young maiden’s name, was made for Hilas, Hilas was made for her.

===Chinese===

“殿下，”塞利姆答道，“史册保证，他试过这条门路，还从经验里悟出：这一性的人，无论何种身份，都不肯做稳赔本的爱情买卖。”
“若当如此，”苏丹说，“我看他这病是无药可救了。”
“他自己同殿下想到一处去了，”塞利姆接着说；“试验屡屡落空，他心灰意冷，一头扎进荒野——这还是依着无数女子的金口：她们明明白白对他说过，他在社交界一无是处。
“他已在荒野里踯躅了好几日，忽听得一处僻静之地传出叹息。他侧耳听，叹息又起，他走近前去，见一位少女，明媚如晨，一手支颋，双眼含泪，余下的身姿也一派沉思哀戚。
“‘夫人，您在这里寻什么？’他对她说。‘这荒野可是为您设的？’
“‘是，’她忧郁地答道：‘在这里，我至少可以痛痛快快地自伤自怜。’
“‘您伤心的缘故是什么？’
“‘唉！’
“‘说吧，夫人，缘故是什么？’
“‘没什么。’
“‘怎么，没什么？’
“‘不，我说了，我的悲哀一无来由：两年前我不幸冒犯了一尊神像，它夺走了我的全部：那东西小得可怜，它下手时，实在没显出多大的神通。打那时起，所有的男人都躲着我，神像说，还要躲下去，直到我遇上一个人，他知道了我的不幸，仍肯同我相守，爱我如今这副模样。’
“‘我听见什么了！’伊拉斯喊道。‘您脚下这个可怜虫，也一无所有，这也正是他的病。前些时他不慎冒犯了一尊神像，被夺走了他原有的一切——不吹牛说，那也真不算小。打那时起，所有的女人都躲着他，神像说，还要躲下去，直到他遇上一个人，她知道了他的不幸，仍肯同他相守，爱他如今这副模样。’
“‘这可能么？’少女说。
“‘您说的可是真话？’伊拉斯说。
“‘请看，’少女答。
“‘请看，’伊拉斯答。
“两人彼此验看，再无疑惑：确是遭了天谴的一对。共同的不幸把他们结在一处。伊菲斯——少女名叫这个——天生是为伊拉斯造的，伊拉斯也是天生为她造的。

===Original===

They loved each other in a platonic manner, as you may easily imagine; for they could not well love otherwise: but in an instant the enchantment was broken, they uttered cries of joy on the event, and platonic love vanished.
“During several months, that they continued together in the desert, they had full time to be persuaded of their change. When they quitted it, Iphis was perfectly cured; but as to Hilas, the author says that he was threatened with a relapse.”

===Chinese===

两人以柏拉图的方式相爱，这不难想见；因为他们也爱不出别的方式：可转瞬之间，魔咒破了，两人为这桩奇事齐声欢呼，柏拉图式的爱情就此消散。
“他们在荒野里厢守了数月，有大把工夫确信各自的变化。离开荒野时，伊菲斯痊愈得十成十；至于伊拉斯，作者说，他那病，倒有复发之虞。”

## LI Thirtieth and Last Trial of the Ring / 指环的第三十次也是最后一次试验
===Original===

While Mangogul was entertaining himself with the favorite and Selim, news was brought him of the death of Sulamek. Sulamek’s rise began by being appointed the Sultan’s dancing-master, and that even contrary to the intentions of Erguebzed: but some intriguing ladies, whom he had taught certain dangerous steps, pushed him with all their might, and so bestirred themselves, that he was preferred to Marcel and others, whose deputy he was not worthy of being. He possessed the spirit of trifling, the court jargon, the gift of telling diverting stories, and of amusing children; but he knew nothing of the high dance. When the post of Grand Vizir became vacant, by dint of cringing, he supplanted the great Seneschal, who was an indefatigable dancer, but stiff-necked, and bowed with a bad grace. His ministry was not signalized by any events glorious to the nation. His enemies, (and who is there without them? true merit has many) accused him of playing ill on the violin, and of having no knowledge of choregraphy; of having suffered himself to be duped by the Pantomimes of Prester John, and to be affrighted by a bear from Monoémugi, who danced one day before him; of having given millions to the emperor of Tombur to keep him from dancing, at a time when he had the gout, and spent yearly five hundred thousand Zecchins in resin, and more in persecuting all the fidlers, who played any other minuets but his own: in a word, of having slept fifteen years of a stretch, at the sound of a cymbal of a clumsy native of Guinea, who accompanied his instrument with some Congo songs. True it is, that he had introduced the fashion of Dutch lime trees, etc.
Mangogul had an excellent heart: he regretted Sulamek, and ordered him a magnificent burial, with a funeral oration, which the orator Brrrouboubou was ordered to make.
On the day appointed for the ceremony, the chief Bramins, the whole Divan in a body, and the Sultanas led by their eunuchs, went to the great mosque. Brrrouboubou proved for two hours together, with surprising rapidity, that Sulamek had risen to his high station by superior talents, made prefaces on prefaces, did not forget either Mangogul or his exploits during the administration of Sulamek; and he had almost spent himself in exclamations, when Mirzoza, to whom a lie gave the vapors, fell into a fit of the lethargy.

===Chinese===

曼戈古尔正陪宠妃和塞利姆取乐，有人来报苏拉梅克的死讯。苏拉梅克的发迹，始于被任为苏丹的舞蹈教师——这甚至违背了埃尔格贝泽德的本意：可几位惯于钻营的夫人，跟他学过几步危险的舞步，拼尽全力往上推他，四下张罗，终于使他压过了马塞尔诸公——给他做副手他都不配的人。他有插科打诲的天分、宫廷切口、说笑话逗孩子的本事；至于高深的舞蹈，他一窍不通。
大维齐尔的位子出缺时，他凭一路的卑躬屈膝，挤掉了那位丞相大臣——大臣是个不知疲倦的舞者，只是脖子硬，行礼欠优雅。他的当政，没有一桩为国增光的大事。他的政敌（谁没有政敌？真才实干的人政敌更多）指控他小提琴拉得糟、对舞谱一无所知；说他被祭司王约翰的哑剧班子耍得团团转，还被莫诺埃穆吉来的一头熊吓得魂飞魄散——那熊有一日在他面前跳了舞；说他送给通布尔皇帝几百万，只为拦着人家跳舞——其时人家正犯着痛风；说他每年花五十万金采齐诺买松香，花更多的钱迫害一切琴师——凡奏别人的小步舞曲而不奏他的，都在被逐之列：一句话，说他伴着几内亚一个笨手笨脚的土人敲铙钹的声响、和着几支刚果歌谣，一觉睡了十五年没醒。不过说句公道话，荷兰菩提树的时兴，确是他带起来的，等等。
曼戈古尔心肠极好：他悼念苏拉梅克，为他下令举办盛大的葬礼，外加一篇悼词，责成演说家布鲁布鲁布鲁撰作。
典礼那日，众位婆罗门首座、全体枢密大臣、还有由太监们引路的众位苏丹妃，齐赴大清真寺。布鲁布鲁布鲁以惊人的语速，一连两个钟头论证苏拉梅克是凭超群的才干升到高位的，前言之后又叠前言，也没忘记曼戈古尔本人及他在苏拉梅克任内的种种功业；他差不多要在连声感叹里耗尽自己了，这时米尔佐莎——谎话最能叫她犯郁症——昏厥了过去。

===Original===

Her officers and women ran to her assistance; she was put into her palanquine, and instantly carried to the Seraglio. Mangogul, being informed of the danger she was in, ran to her; and the whole Pharmacopoeia was rummaged. The Garus, general la Motte’s drops, the English drops, were all tried, but to no purpose. The distressed Sultan, one moment weeping over Mirzoza, the next swearing at Orcotomus, at length was deprived of all hopes, except some glimmerings which might remain in his ring. “If I have lost you, delight of my soul,” cried he, “your Toy, as well as your mouth, must keep eternal silence.”
In an instant he commanded everybody to withdraw: he was obeyed; and being alone with the favorite, he turned his ring on her. But Mirzoza’s Toy, which was tired at the sermon, as it happens to others every day, and besides had probably a touch of the lethargy, at first only mutter’d some confused and ill articulated words. The Sultan repeated the experiment, and the Toy then very distinctly said: “Separated from you, Mangogul, what would become of me?﻿—Faithful even to the grave, I would have sought you; and if love and constancy have any recompence among the dead, dear prince, I would have found you﻿—Alas! without you the delightful palace where Brama dwells, and which he has promised to his true believers, would prove a disagreeable residence to me.”
Mangogul, transported with joy, did not perceive that the favorite was recovering from her lethargy; and that, if he did not turn off his ring, she would hear the last words of her Toy: which happened accordingly. “Ah! prince,” said she, “what is become of your oaths? You have then cleared up your unjust suspicions? Nothing has withheld you, neither the condition I was in, the injury you were doing me, nor the word you had given me?”
“Madam,” replied the Sultan, “impute not to a shameful curiosity, an impatience, which the despair for having lost you, has alone suggested to me. It was not an usual trial of my ring, that I made on you; but I believed that I might, without breach of promise, make use of a resource, which restores you to my vows, and secures my heart to you forever.”
“Prince,” says the favorite, “I believe you; but pray restore the ring to the Genius, and let not his fatal present disturb your heart or empire for the future.”
Mangogul immediately made a prayer, and Cucufa appeared.

===Chinese===

她的官员和侍女们奔来救护；她被抬上轿子，即刻送回后宫。曼戈古尔听说她危在旦夕，飞奔而去；整部药典都被翻了个底朝天。盖鲁斯药水、拉莫特将军药露、英国药露，一一试过，全不见效。悲恸的苏丹，这一刻伏在米尔佐莎身上垂泪，下一刻冲着奥科托穆斯破口大骂，末了只剩一线指望——或许还残存在他的指环里。“我心灵的欢愉，我若失去了你，”他喊道，“你的玩具，便只好同你的嘴一样，永远缄默了。”
他即刻命众人退下：众人从命；他单独同宠妃待在一起，朝她转动了指环。可米尔佐莎的玩具，在布道会上听乏了——旁人天天如此——况且多半也染了点昏睡症，起初只咕哝了几句含混不清的话。苏丹再试了一次，玩具这才字字清晰地开口：“同您分离了，曼戈古尔，我会怎样呢？——我到坟墓里也忠贞不渝，我会去寻您；若恩爱和坚贞在死者那里也有报偿，亲爱的殿下，我准能寻见您——唉！没有您，梵天居住的、许给真信徒的那座欢乐宫殿，于我也不过是处难耐的居所。”
曼戈古尔喜不自胜，竟没觉察宠妃正从昏睡中醒来；也没觉察，若不关上指环，她便会听见她的玩具最后的几句：而这恰恰发生了。“啊！殿下，”她说，“您的誓言都到哪里去了？您那番无端的猜疑，就算澄清了？什么都不再拦着您了——不管我正处于什么境地，不管您怎样伤害我，也不管您对我许下的诺言？”
“夫人，”苏丹答道，“请别把这一回的急切，归罪于可耻的好奇心；逼出这急切的，唯有失去您的绝望。我对您做的，不是指环寻常的试验；我以为，动用这最后一线凭藉，算不得背誓——它把您还给了我的誓愿，也把我的心永远拴在了您身上。”
“殿下，”宠妃说，“我相信您；可请把指环还给精灵吧，莫叫他这件致命的礼物，日后再揽扰您的心，揽扰您的帝国。”
曼戈古尔当即作了一祷，库库法现身了。

===Original===

“Almighty Genius,” said Mangogul, “take back your ring, and continue me your protection.”
“Prince,” answered the Genius, “divide your days between love and glory. Mirzoza will certainly afford you the first of these blessings; and I promise you the second.”
At these words the hooded spectre closed the tails of his owls, and went off, as he came, in a whirling motion.

===Chinese===

“全能的精灵，”曼戈古尔说，“收回您的指环吧，并请继续庇佑于我。”
“殿下，”精灵答道，“把您的岁月分给爱情和荣光。米尔佐莎必能给您前一样福分；后一样，我许给您。”
话音方落，这戴兜帽的幽灵合拢他双鸮的尾羽，来时怎样旋转而来，去时便怎样旋转而去。

3. A hill near Paris famous for asses. ↩︎

2. 我的运气好到这般地步，连我自己也几乎不敢相信。 ↩︎
